After **The Penetration of Tinctures** <note>original: "La pénétration des teintures" — In alchemy, a "tincture" is a potent substance believed to be capable of "penetrating" and transforming the nature of metals or medicines.</note> by **Basil Valentine** <note>A legendary figure in the history of alchemy, supposedly a 15th-century Benedictine monk, though his influential works began appearing in print around 1600.</note> follows **The Vegetable Water** <note>A "vegetable water" (eau vegetalle) was typically a medicinal distillate made from plant matter, used for various healing purposes.</note> of **Father Chotay**, a Jesuit, from the time he was on a mission at Caen in 1677, which he had printed and given to the public.

Next follows the **Mineral or Health Water** of **Mr. Lefebvre Lubin**, of which he has provided such proof and which had such great sales in Paris that it is called **Fabor’s Water** <note>original: "L'eau de fabor" — This is a phonetic nickname or colloquial shortening of the inventor's name, Le Febure (meaning "The Smith").</note>.

# REVELATION
## OF THE MYSTERIES
# OF THE TINCTURES
-> Essential to the SEVEN METALS, <note>In alchemy, the "Seven Metals" correspond to the seven celestial bodies: Gold (Sun), Silver (Moon), Copper (Venus), Iron (Mars), Tin (Jupiter), Lead (Saturn), and Quicksilver (Mercury).</note> <-
-> & of their MEDICINAL *Virtues*. <note>original: "Vertus" — In this context, "virtues" refers to the inherent healing powers or properties of the substances.</note> <-

-> Composed in German by F. BASIL VALENTINE, <-
-> *Religious of the Order of Saint Benedict.* <note>Basil Valentine is a legendary figure; while stories place him as a 15th-century monk, many scholars believe these works were written by his "translator" or editor, Johann Thölde, around 1600.</note> <-

-> And translated by the Lord I. ISRAEL, German Physician. <-

-> *Dedicated to Monsieur* VAUTIER, *Councilor to the King in his Councils* <-
-> *of State and Private, and First Physician to His Majesty.* <note>François Vautier (1589–1652) was a powerful physician who served both Marie de' Medici and King Louis XIV; he was known for his interest in chemical medicines like antimony.</note> <-

### Divided into SEVEN Chapters.

- Center right: a figure in robes and a turban labeled "Hermes Trismegistus, Eastern Physician." <note>original: "Hermes Trismegistus Orientalis Phis." — Hermes Trismegistus is the mythical founder of Hermeticism and alchemy.</note> holding a celestial sphere and standing near a furnace with glass alembics.

This suggests that music and chemical harmony can drive away illness or "evil spirits."</note>

# TO MONSIEUR
## MONSIEUR VAUTIER,
### COUNSELOR TO THE KING,
->in his Councils of State and Privy,<-
->AND<-
->First Physician to His Majesty.<-

SIR,

In the short time that I have had the good fortune to be in France (after having been in England and in Holland), where the calamity of the wars occurring in my country of Germany <note>The author is referring to the Thirty Years' War (1618–1648), which caused a significant migration of Protestant scholars and physicians across Europe.</note> forced me to seek a sanctuary, so that I might with greater tranquility practice the study of both types of <term>Medicine</term> <note>The "two medicines" typically refers to the traditional Galenic (herbal/humoral) system and the emerging Spagyric (chemical/alchemical) system favored by Paracelsians.</note>, which I profess; I have made an observation that seems to me worthy of astonishment, in that one still encounters many people,

<summary>A German physician and refugee of the Thirty Years' War dedicates his work to François Vautier, the First Physician to the King of France. The author notes his arrival in France after traveling through England and Holland to seek a peaceful environment for his medical studies.</summary>
<keywords>François Vautier, Thirty Years' War, medical education, Galenic medicine, Spagyric medicine, Royal Physician, 17th-century medicine, refugees</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the dedicatory epistle to François Vautier. The author is describing the skepticism he has encountered in France regarding alchemical medicine.</meta>

# EPISTLE.

of whom one part is composed of the ignorant and the common people; and the other of a more elevated condition, who have spent several years in the public Academies in the study of Philosophy. These individuals, nevertheless, do not cease to disparage the admirable effects of the Remedies that can be derived from <term>HERMETIC Medicine</term> <note>Also known as Spagyric medicine or alchemy, this system—championed by Paracelsus—sought to extract the "essential" healing properties of minerals and plants through chemical processes like distillation and sublimation.</note>, and particularly dare to maintain that the preparation of a <term>Universal MEDICINE</term> <note>The "Panacea," a legendary substance believed to cure all diseases and restore youth.</note>—which the Philosophers have called their <term>Physical Stone</term> <note>More commonly known as the Philosopher's Stone; the author uses "Physical" in the archaic sense of "relating to nature or medicine."</note>—could never have been found, and can still only be encountered in the fantasy and "hollow brains," as they say, of a few dreamers and melancholics <note>In the 17th century, "melancholy" was not just sadness but a physiological imbalance thought to cause delusions or obsessive "madness."</note>.

I must confess to you that I have only been able to endure the slander of both groups with the greatest impatience; but especially that of the latter, who make a profession of doctrine. This has often made me marvel all the more at the order of **Divine Providence**, for allowing such people—who are entirely unworthy of the knowledge of the Wonders of **NATURE**—to be struck with such blindness that they have been unable to discover any ray of those great lights of **Antiquity**, nor of those that still exist in this Century,

<meta>catchword: which</meta>

<summary>The author expresses his frustration with critics—both the uneducated and the academic elite—who dismiss Hermetic medicine and the possibility of the Philosopher's Stone as mere delusions. He views their skepticism as a form of divine blindness that prevents the "unworthy" from accessing the deep secrets of nature.</summary>
<keywords>Hermetic medicine, Universal Medicine, Philosopher's Stone, academic skepticism, Divine Providence, 17th-century philosophy, alchemy, Spagyric remedies</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the dedicatory epistle to François Vautier. The author is transitioning from describing the skepticism of the French academies to citing historical authorities and contemporary practitioners who validate alchemical medicine.</meta>

->### EPISTLE.<-

...which provide us the means to penetrate into this admirable **TREASURE** of <term>Natural Philosophy</term> <note>The study of nature and the physical universe, which was the precursor to modern science.</note>.

*How many past centuries have provided us with learned men of considerable authority who—not wishing to hide their light under a bushel <note>A biblical reference to Matthew 5:15, meaning they chose to share their knowledge rather than keep it secret.</note>—have left us very solemn and learned traditions in favor of **CHEMISTRY** <note>During this period, "chemistry" or "chymistry" included both practical laboratory techniques and the spiritual/theoretical goals of alchemy.</note>, and especially concerning the admirable preparation it can provide of its **universal MEDICINE**, as much for the perfect **HEALTH** of Animals and Plants as even for Minerals and Metals.*

*How many other witnesses to this Truth have the recent centuries also left us? And how many of the most learned and rare men could still be found at present who would not hesitate to stand as guarantors of this Truth? If it were not for the fear they have of profaning this precious **GIFT** of **GOD**, they could allow the Reality of it to be seen and touched—the same Reality which they use daily in several parts of the world, and particularly in our country of **Germany**.*

It is this which has made me undertake the translation

<summary>The author argues that history and contemporary German practitioners provide ample evidence for the "Universal Medicine," suggesting that practitioners often hide their knowledge only to avoid "profaning" a divine gift.</summary>

->### EPISTLE.<-

*of one of the most significant authors that our own country has produced in these recent centuries, whose religious life and zeal for sincerity and charity toward his neighbor cannot be suspected of bad faith; when he swears to have said nothing that he has not seen, touched, and experienced himself, and to have described the circumstances as plainly* <note>The original French "naïvement" here means "naturally" or "candidly," implying a lack of artifice or deception.</note> *as possible for him, and as much as* **GOD** *has permitted him for the instruction and consolation of the Children of* **SCIENCE** <note>A term often used by alchemists to refer to themselves as "Sons of Art" or "Children of Knowledge," those initiated into the secrets of nature.</note>, *and for the conviction of the ignorant or presumptuous skeptics and persecutors, who vomit a thousand insults against this* **TRUTH** *of* **CHEMISTRY.**

*After these motives, I believed that nothing remained for me but to make the fortunate encounter with a person who could prevent slander by the testimony of their knowledge of the Truths contained in the works of* **BASIL VALENTINE**: <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist and Benedictine monk whose writings were highly influential in early modern medicine.</note>
*You have (* **SIR** *) a complete reading of all the good authors of this Philosophy; all the great insights and* **CHEMICAL** *experiments by which you have made yourself so admirable in our profession, and so highly esteemed at Court and by the people, that when it was*

<summary>The translator justifies his choice of Basil Valentine as a reliable author based on the monk's piety and firsthand experience, and begins addressing the dedicatee as a peerless expert in chemical philosophy.</summary>

### EPISTLE.

*a question of choosing someone who deserved the post of First Physician to the King* <note>The "Premier Médecin du Roy" was the head of the royal medical household and the most powerful medical figure in France, often holding great influence over which new medicines were permitted.</note>, *your person was elected by public consensus; this leads me to hope that your approval will silence those who condemn what their minds cannot understand.*

*If you do me the honor of accepting this Treatise, I shall give to the public others by the same Author which have not yet been seen in France, from which they will receive much satisfaction and utility; and I shall seek more significant opportunities to show you that I am,*

->**SIR,**<-

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the translator’s formal dedication to the King’s First Physician into a preface written by the printer. While the previous page focused on the political and medical legitimacy of Basil Valentine’s work, this page introduces the symbolic and visual structure of the book itself, framed as a "Chemical Theater."</meta>

# THE PRINTER
## TO THE READER.

Friend READER,

My plan being to show you the decoration of a **CHEMICAL THEATER**, upon which appear as Actors only persons of irreproachable Authority in the knowledge of the Wonders of **TYPOGRAPHY**—or the printing of signatures <note>In 17th-century printing, "signatures" were letters or numbers at the bottom of pages used to guide the binder in folding the sheets correctly.</note>—and of the *HARMONY* or universal balanced temperament of all the most curious things that are in Nature, especially concerning the preparation of the great **ELIXIR** or universal **MEDICINE** of the **HERMETIC Philosophers** <note>Followers of Hermes Trismegistus, referring here to alchemists seeking the "Philosopher's Stone" or a universal cure.</note>.

I intend to find the arrangement for this undertaking within my own Mark or harmonic and Typographic **ESCUTCHEON** <note>A printer's "mark" or device was a decorative emblem used as a trademark on the title page.</note>, from which I have drawn the design of a small figure that I placed on the frontispiece of this **BOOK** in favor of the **SEVEN** Authors noted therein, and particularly of **BASIL VALENTINE** and **HERMES Trismegistus**, one of whom is from the West and the other from the East, and who I hope shall be the two principal Guardians of this aforementioned Mark or **ESCUTCHEON**.

The decoration of this **THEATER** will consist firstly of **SEVEN** varied Columns, each with the Color and particular circumstances of the harmonic Relations that can be observed in the Blazon <note>Heraldic description or symbolic coat of arms.</note> of each of the **SEVEN** Metals.

This same **THEATER** shall be illuminated by **SEVEN** principal Crystal Chandeliers, each surrounded by several small brilliant stones; of all the various Colors of Minerals and marcasites <note>A term then used for various metallic minerals, often iron pyrites, believed to contain the "seeds" of metals.</note> that symbolize each of the **SEVEN** aforementioned Metals, which will represent the shape of the Characters <note>The symbolic shorthand or glyphs used in alchemy and astrology.</note> of the **SEVEN** Planets, upon which depend the various Celestial Bodies and Constellations, each composed of several Stars.

<summary>The printer introduces the book's symbolic layout, comparing the publication to a "Chemical Theater" organized around the number seven, representing the seven metals, planets, and primary alchemical authors.</summary>
<keywords>Chemical Theater, Typography, Alchemy, Elixir, Basil Valentine, Hermes Trismegistus, Seven Metals, Seven Planets, printer's mark, marcasite</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the printer’s preface, expanding on the metaphorical "Chemical Theater" introduced on the previous page. It details the specific alchemical authors who act as the "performers" and describes the musical accompaniment that structures the work’s seven-part division.</meta>

The **Actors** of the first **Seven**-day cycle <note>The original French "Sept-maine" is a play on "semaine" (week), implying a seven-part structure mirroring the seven days of creation.</note> will be the **Seven Authors** marked in the said figure on the frontispiece of this **Book**, who are **Hermes**, Geber, Raymond Lully, Artephius, Basil Valentine, Flamel, & the Cosmopolite; among whom there will be two who will begin the Opening of this **Theater**, namely Basil Valentine, & **Hermes Trismegistus**, Prince of Philosophers.

Each of these **Authors** will make **Seven** theatrical entrances *which will compose the* **Seven** *scenes of this theater, through which you will hear wonders regarding the subject of the* **Seven** *preparations that I have marked by* **Seven** *vials which are represented in the aforementioned figure of the frontispiece mentioned above, and regarding the explanation of the* **Seven Characters** <note>In this context, "characters" refers to alchemical symbols or signs.</note> *affixed upon them.*

This first **Seven**-day cycle will begin on Wednesday in favor of the **Mercury** of the Philosophers <note>In alchemy, Wednesday is traditionally associated with the planet and element Mercury.</note>, and because **Mercury**, *following the opinion of the Ancients in their Mythology, is the inventor of* **Music** *which will be played at each intermission, the concert of which, being for two choirs—namely one from the East and the other from the West—will be composed of* **Seven voices** *and* **Seven instruments;** the two principal ones of which will be the Bass **Viol** with **Seven Strings**, and **a Harpsichord** organized by the repetitions and doublings of the **Diapason** <term>Originally referring to the interval of an octave, here it describes the full range or compass</term> of the **Seven** pipes which are represented in the small figure alleged above. If I have enough good fortune (Friend Reader) to receive testimonies of your approval for this design of mine, I can assure you that I will apply all possible diligence to bring to light the rest of the **Seven** manuscript treatises that I have by Basil Valentine, translated into Latin from his works printed in German, which have not yet been seen in either Latin or French; these I promise you in French translation. In this translation, I also hope to make appear to you—in a style much more intelligible than has been done before—the true sentiments of **Hermes Trismegistus** in his Arabic treatise on the Revelation of the **Seven Egyptian Seals**, which may be followed by **Seventy** Treatises by this Prince of the Magi or Philosophers of the East, since it is held that he wrote in...

<summary>The printer introduces the seven alchemical masters who serve as "actors" in this book and explains the musical and symbolic structure of the "Seven-day cycle," centered on the figure of Mercury. He also promises future translations of rare works by Basil Valentine and Hermes Trismegistus.</summary>

<keywords>Seven-day cycle, Chemical Theater, Hermes Trismegistus, Basil Valentine, Alchemy, Mercury, Seven Seals, Alchemical Music</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of authors intended for the "Chemical Theater." It transitions from the foundational Greek and Arabic sources to the Latin scholastic alchemists like Raymond Lully and the more recent mystical-chemical works of the German doctor Heinrich Khunrath, maintaining the "Seven-part" organizational structure established previously.</meta>

...Arabic, more than three hundred volumes to complete the perfect Course of **Philosophical Medicine**, which is called **Hermetic**; in memory of that rare Interpreter of natural and supernatural Philosophy. <note>The author refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational alchemical and philosophical texts were attributed.</note>

After this, following the favorable reception you give to the two aforementioned Authors; I may also put to the Press several treatises by the rest of the **Seven** who appear in the said figure; among whom I hope that this admirable **Genius** of the enlightened Martyr and Monk **Raymond Lully**, that Prince of the most learned Western Philosophers—who is also held to have written in Latin more than three hundred volumes on the **universal Medicine** of the Philosophers—will be favorable to me in showing you, through a selection of about **Seventy** of his finest treatises, all that one could wish for in the marvelous effects of true **Chemistry**, and I may begin with his treatise on the **Seven Wheels** of Philosophy. <note>Raymond Lully (c. 1232–1315) was a Majorcan philosopher; though many alchemical works were falsely attributed to him, they remained highly influential in the 17th century.</note>

And if I am permitted, I shall add yet more works by several other Authors whose singular Doctrine has made them commendable in both branches of **Medicine**, such as, among others, one **Heinrich Khunrath**, whom Germany has produced for us, from whom I may bring to light **Seven** treatises which have not been seen in French, namely the *Amphitheater*, the *Apocalypse*, the treatise on **Fire**, on **Magnesia** <term>Magnesia: In alchemy, this does not refer to the modern element magnesium, but rather to the "primitive matter" or the mineral substance used to begin the Great Work.</term>, on the **Athanor** <term>Athanor: A furnace used by alchemists designed to maintain a uniform, constant heat over long periods.</term>, on the **Symbol**, and on the **Confession**.

The first of these treatises teaches the means to attain Eternal Wisdom through **Seven** (what this Author calls) **Theosophical** degrees <note>Theosophical: From the Greek for "divine wisdom"; a mystical approach to understanding God and the universe through nature.</note> by means of which one must arrive at the door of this **Amphitheater** which is divided into **Seven Cells**, in honor of the **Seven** mottos or **Seven** laws of the Oracle which he calls **Christian-Kabbalistic**, **Divine-magical**, and **Physico-Chemical**. In short, to complete this **Chemical Theater** and leave nothing to be desired in the proofs of the truths of Hermetic Philosophy, I may still, in my hours of leisure, compile in **Seven-week** cycles <note>Original: "Sept-maines." A recurring pun in this text, playing on the French "semaine" (week) to emphasize a seven-part organizational structure.</note>, and join to this plan the most significant treatises of **Arnaldus de Villa Nova**, of **John of Rupescissa**, of the...

<summary>The author outlines plans to publish a massive collection of alchemical works, focusing on Raymond Lully and Heinrich Khunrath, structured around the symbolic number seven. These works are presented as a complete course in "Hermetic Medicine" and "Physico-Chemical" truths.</summary>
<keywords>Raymond Lully, Heinrich Khunrath, Hermetic Medicine, Athanor, Magnesia, Theosophy, Chemical Theater, Arnaldus de Villa Nova, John of Rupescissa</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of alchemical authorities, picking up from the catchword "HARMONIE" (Harmony) which links the medicinal arts to the mechanical art of printing. It connects the "Seven-part" structure of the previous pages to a long list of historical masters and concludes with a philosophical justification for the art of typography itself.</meta>

Count Bernard of Trevisan, of Morienus, of Isaac the Dutchman, Paracelsus, Khalid, Albumasar, Rachaidibi, Hali, Senior, Petrus Bonus, Rosinus, William, and Christopher of Paris, Polyphile, Rhazes, Synesius, Damascene, Avicenna, Plato, Jean de Meung, Ripley, Augurello, Dastin, Bacon, Pontanus, Zacaire, Agricola, the Prince of Mirandola and others; <note>This list includes a "who's who" of alchemy spanning from legendary Greek and Arabic figures to Medieval and Renaissance scholars like Jean de Meung (author of the *Romance of the Rose*) and the polymath Pico della Mirandola.</note> among whom some have treated the SEVEN Little Keys, <note>original: "Clauicules"</note> *or* SEVEN *Oaks of Philosophy, others the* SEVEN *Keys of Paradise; another composed the* Mirror of Alchemy divided into SEVEN *Chapters, of which the* SEVEN *initial letters express in sequence the name of one of the* SEVEN *Hermetic Planets.* And if I find that people remain curious to see more of the beautiful research found in the works of Michael Maier, <note>original: "Majerus"</note> *I may print* SEVEN *treatises by him, the first of which is titled* Philosophical Week. <note>original: "Septimana Philosophica," a 1620 work by Maier that uses the structure of the seven days of creation to explain alchemical principles.</note>

Finally, to conclude according to my first and particular design in favor of TYPOGRAPHY and HARMONY, I hope that the Curious and meditative Reader will be able to easily conjecture by reading the following BOOK, that the invention and practice of the noble Art of TYPOGRAPHY is entirely Philosophical, once it pleases him to reflect upon the prerogatives and high advantages that BASIL VALENTINE <note>Basil Valentine was a legendary alchemist; the author here links Valentine's theories on metals to the tools of the printing trade.</note> attributes to the Hermetic MARS and VENUS, who may be considered the Agent and the Patient of the Philosophers; and when he wishes to place them in Parallel with the various circumstances of the manufacture and use of the Prototypes or Punches of PRINTING, *which being made of Steel symbolize* MARS, *and the Matrices which, being made of copper, are one and the same thing as* VENUS: furthermore, the Character Molds <note>The molds used to cast individual pieces of metal type.</note> deserve to be compared to the vessels in which the hermetic generation occurs. And specifically, it may be observed that the *SECRET of the metallic transmutation of* SATURN <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the symbol for Lead.</note> *of which our Author speaks at the end of this Treatise, is practiced in the Philosophical and Typo-chemical casting* <note>original: "Typochuſique." A neologism combining "Typography" and the Greek "cheuo" (to pour/cast), implying that casting type is a form of alchemy.</note> *that one must perform upon this same* SATURN, *which is the base of the material for the said Characters and* PRINTING PLATES.

<summary>The author lists numerous alchemical authorities who structured their work around the number seven and argues that the art of printing is itself a "philosophical" or alchemical process. He draws direct parallels between printing materials (steel, copper, lead) and alchemical principles (Mars, Venus, Saturn).</summary>

<keywords>alchemical authors, Seven Keys, Michael Maier, Basil Valentine, typography as alchemy, Mars and Venus, Saturn, metallic transmutation, typo-chemical casting</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's philosophical justification for typography, picking up from the previous page's conclusion where the art of printing was framed as a philosophical endeavor. It specifically connects the materials of printing—ink and paper—to alchemical processes.</meta>

Finally, if one further observes what is described in this **BOOK**
concerning the *Printing Paper*, it will be found that the sequence
of *Philosophical* operations is exactly represented therein; and
regarding the *Physical* mysteries which are hidden in the process
used to make the *Ink* with which one *Prints*,
I hope to show through other subsequent treatises what
several serious *Authors* have written about the *Solvent* or *Oil of the*
*Philosophers*, which is the *Oily Varnish* in which they
performed the dissolution of what they called *blacker than*
*black* <note>original: "nigrum nigrius nigro," a common alchemical term for the "nigredo" or blackening stage, the first step in the Great Work.</note>: from which the learned *Ramon Llull* <note>original: "Raymond-Lulle," a 13th-century Majorcan polymath whose name was frequently attached to later alchemical works.</note> likely
had occasion to compose the treatise titled *Treatise on*
*Inks* <note>original: "Tractatus Atramentorum"</note>: so that it is not without reason if I dare maintain and desire
to prove more fully that it is from this learned **Art of PRINT-**
**ING** that the *Curious* <note>In the 17th century, "the Curious" referred to a community of scholars and virtuosos interested in experimental science and hidden knowledge.</note> should hope for more natural *figures* and
the most perfect *Types* of the admirable mysteries of the most
*Curious Physiology* <note>In this period, "Physiology" referred generally to the study of nature and its inner workings, often including alchemy and physics.</note>; indeed even of the most
contemplative *Theology*.

And regarding **HARMONY**; whether one considers
that of sounds called **MUSIC**; or even that of the relations
and exact justifications <note>In printing, "justification" is the process of adjusting the space between words so that the lines of type are of equal length and align at the margins.</note> of the *Characters* and **PLATES** of
a beautiful and *Correct Printing*; without describing here the *Cu-*
*rious* reasonings of the incomparable *Ramon Llull* and
others: I will content myself with saying in general terms that one
cannot hope for a more remarkable hieroglyph to represent
the **HARMONY** of true *Logic* which teaches the true
agreements of one or more attributes with a subject, in which con-
sist the true knowledge of all the University faculties
and of all the most exalted sciences, and particularly the **SYM-**
**PHONY** of the *Magnetic Inclinations* of the *elements* or *Prin-*
*ciples*, and of the qualities or temperaments of all the *Com-*
*pounds* <note>original: "Mix-tes," referring to any material substance formed by the combination of the four elements.</note> which are in the **U N I V E R S E**; and (especially that of the various
*Characters* of the *Passions*; or of the *Philosophical Matters*, the
*Melody* and peaceful accord of which is the principal end
of all those who exercise themselves in the study of *moral* **THEOLOGY**
and of the *universal* **PHYSIOLOGY** of the **HERMETIC**
*Philosophers*.

<summary>The author argues that the physical materials of printing—specifically ink and paper—are metaphors for alchemical processes, citing Ramon Llull as an authority. He further links the harmony of musical sound and typographical alignment to the "symphony" of the natural and moral world.</summary>
<keywords>Typography, Alchemy, Ramon Llull, Hermetic Philosophy, Logic, Harmony, Nigredo, Printing Ink</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the physical arts—specifically printing and distillation—are earthly mirrors of spiritual and alchemical processes. Having previously linked ink-making to the alchemical "nigredo" stage, he now introduces the "tinctures" or colors of the Great Work and connects them to his other published works on the chemistry of wine and the nature of metals.</meta>

This treatise on **TINCTURES** <note>original: "TEINTVRES." In alchemy, a "tincture" is a potent substance capable of transmuting or "coloring" base metals into gold or silver, or a medicinal extract.</note> will be able to make the *Curious Reader* a lover of the two principal Colors of **HERMETIC Philosophy**, which he will observe through this book to aspire only to the white, or to the red <note>These represent the "Albedo" (whiteness) and "Rubedo" (redness), the final two stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note>; and if, after this, he falls into the opinion of many who believe that the **CLUSTER** of grapes is one of the most significant materials of these Philosophers—seeing as the **JUICE** of this **CLUSTER** possesses naturally and by preference one or the other of these two colors—I invite him to cherish their use, and to desire to see another book that I have newly finished Printing, which treats very curiously of the theoretical and practical **ANATOMY of WINE**, and of the advantageous use of its alcoholized **BRANDY**, which the Philosophers call their **HEAVEN**, in which can be placed all the Planets and constellations of **lower Astronomy**, and particularly these two great Luminaries of the **Hermetic SUN** and **MOON**, commonly called potable **GOLD** and **SILVER**, whose virtues are so admirable and so effective that almost nothing is impossible in this world to him who possesses this **TREASURE**. Thus, the curious Reader may, if he pleases, observe at greater length in another Treatise that I printed a short time ago on the **lower Astronomy** of the **SEVEN Metals**, and on the **HARMONY** of their systems; together with the twelve signs of the Zodiac, and other constellations of the **HEAVEN** of the **HERMETIC Philosophers**.

<meta>This page transitions from the author's philosophical introduction regarding wine and alchemy to the legal and technical front matter of the book. It provides the official royal authorization for the publication and a list of corrections for printing errors, anchoring the esoteric content in the physical reality of 17th-century Parisian publishing.</meta>

# EXTRACT OF THE PRIVILEGE <note>A "Privilege" was a form of early copyright granted by the King, giving an author or printer exclusive rights to publish a work for a set period.</note>

By the grace and Privilege of the King, permission is granted to Master I.I., a German Physician, to have printed, sold, and distributed, by such Printer and Bookseller as he shall advise, the translation of a Treatise on **TINCTURES** <note>original: "TEINTVRES." In alchemy, a tincture is a potent substance or medicine capable of transmuting base metals or healing the body.</note> by **Frater Basil Valentine**, etc. during the time and space of six years beginning from the day it shall be finished printing: and all other Printers and Booksellers, other than those having the power and consent of the said I.I., are forbidden from printing or selling the said Book, upon pain of confiscation of the counterfeit copies under whatever pretext it may be, and of five hundred livres fine <note>The "livre" was the currency of France until 1795.</note> payable without deposit, costs, damages, and interests, etc. Given at Paris; the eleventh day of February 1645. Signed, by the King in his Council, DENISOT, and sealed with the Great Seal of yellow wax.

And the said Master I. I. has ceded and transferred the aforementioned privilege to J. de Senlecque, Engraver and Typefounder of PRINTING PLATES <note>Jacques de Senlecque was a famous French typefounder and printer known for his technical innovations in typography.</note>, Printer and Bookseller in Paris, to enjoy according to its form and tenor.

->The printing of this Book was completed on September 7, 1645.<-

---

### *Errors occurring during printing.*

Page 4. line 5. *its maintenance of the spirit; its Matter of the body, and its form of the Soul.* read: *its substance is animated, its Matter is spiritual, and its form is earthly.* <note>These corrections reflect the precise alchemical definitions of the three parts of a substance: the Soul, the Spirit, and the Body.</note>

The Reader is prayed to be willing to supply the correction if he encounters some slight spelling error; whether by transposition, omission, or the unexpected addition of some letter which does not change the sense of the discourse.

<meta>This page contains the legal publishing permissions (Privilege) and the errata (corrections) for the French translation of a work by the alchemist Basil Valentine. It provides a specific timeline for the book's production in 1645.</meta>

# EXTRACT OF THE PRIVILEGE

By the grace and Privilege of the King, it is permitted to the Lord I.I. <note>likely Jean Isaäc, a physician often associated with alchemical translations of this period</note>, German Physician, to have printed, sold, and distributed by such Printer and Bookseller as he shall advise, the translation of a *Treatise on* TINCTURES <note>original: "Teintures"; in alchemy, these are elixirs or substances capable of transforming metals or curing disease</note> *of Brother Basil Valentine*, etc. during the time and space of six years starting from the day it shall be finished printing: and prohibitions are made to all other Printers and Booksellers, other than those having the power and consent of the said I.I., to print or sell the said Book, under penalty of confiscation of the counterfeit copies under whatever pretext it may be, and of five hundred livres fine payable without delay, along with costs, damages, and interests etc. Given at Paris; the eleventh day of February 1645. Signed, by the King in his Council, DENISOT, and sealed with the great seal of yellow wax.

And the said Lord I.I. has ceded and transferred the aforementioned privilege to J. de Senlecque, Engraver and Type-founder of PRINTING PLATES <note>literally "Printing Plates," but referring here to his profession as a founder of the metal type used in printing</note>, Printer and Bookseller in Paris, to enjoy according to its form and tenor.

->The printing of this Book was completed on the 7th of SEPTEMBER 1645.<-

---

### *Errors occurred during printing.*

Page 4. line 5. *its maintenance of the spirit, its Matter of the body, & its form of the Soul.* read: *its substance is animated, its Matter is spiritual, & its form is terrestrial.* <note>These corrections reflect the specific alchemical terminology regarding the three-fold nature of substances (Spirit, Soul, and Body)</note>

The Reader is asked to please provide a correction if they encounter any slight error of spelling; whether by transposition, omission, or the unexpected addition of some letter that does not change the meaning of the discourse.

# Treatise on Natural and Supernatural Things

## Concerning the first tincture, the Root and spirit of metals and minerals, and how they are Contained, Born, engendered, and of Truth

---

->By Brother Basil Valentine of<-
->the Order of Saint Benedict<-

->On things natural and supernatural<-

### Chapter 1.

Since I have set out to discuss and bring to light the original <term>tincture</term> <note>In alchemical philosophy, a "tincture" refers to the potent essence or spiritual force capable of transforming and "coloring" base metals into a more perfect state, such as gold.</note> of metals and minerals, and to reveal for common knowledge how metals and minerals are, at the beginning, contained spiritually by nature, and then engendered and produced, I have deemed it fitting to first make you understand through this discourse which...

<summary>This opening page introduces a treatise by the Benedictine monk Basil Valentine concerning the spiritual origins and physical formation of metals and minerals. It establishes the author's intent to reveal the "original tincture" or essence of these substances to a wider audience.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Basil Valentine, metals, minerals, tincture, spiritual nature, Order of Saint Benedict, transmutation</keywords>

**Supernatural** [are] all things **magical**

For it rests there peacefully, performing its operations in such a manner...

### Natural

Now, this spirit is found in all metals and can be drawn and extracted more abundantly from all other metals than from gold, because gold is a body too hard and strongly fixed <term>Fixed: In alchemy, "fixed" (figé) describes a substance that is stable and resistant to fire or change, whereas "volatile" substances evaporate or transform easily.</term>, too solid and compact. This is why this spirit is so closely and necessarily joined and bound with this perfect body that it is almost inseparable from it. But the other metals do not have such a fixed and tight body; instead, their structure is much more widely open. It is for this reason that the spirit or tincture enters there more easily; and because they are inconstant and not very firm, the tincture cannot remain with them; thus, it can be removed with ease and without much trouble. However, because the tincture of gold is not as abundant as that of other metals—

---

For when it comes to Animals, it operates by itself; regarding *vegetables*, it works in another way through itself; as also with *Metals & Minerals*; each one nourishing itself according to its particular instinct, regarding which, if I wished, I would have cause to write very ample discourses.

One would have reason to *ask me with good cause* how one could have or make this *Mercurial Spirit*? In what way it must be prepared, so that it might heal diseases, and *transmute imperfect Metals by their own seed*? a *Ruby*, pour it over some well-calcined white TARTAR <note>The residue found in wine casks (potassium bitartrate), purified by extreme heat (calcination).</note>,
and distill the whole over a sufficiently strong
ash fire, and the spirit of WINE will remain with the TARTAR:
but the *Spirit of MERCURY* will pass over <note>The "spirit" here refers to the volatile essence being distilled off into the receiving vessel.</note>.

If you mix this *Spirit of MERCURY* with the *sulfurous*
*spirit of the* SUN <term>In alchemy, the "Sun" (Sol) refers to Gold.</term>, and with its SALT, and if you
can join them together by suitable distillation,
so that they never separate one
from the other, then you will have a *Medicine* sin-
gularly excellent: But if you ferment this
*Medicine* with the body of the SUN, according to the
required weight, and you bring it to perfection by
perfect *decoction* <term>The process of heating a substance in a sealed vessel for an extended period to "ripen" or mature it.</term> for a certain time, then
you will have a GOLD more than perfect, which will be a sov-
ereign *Medicine*, as much for diseases as for
poverty, and you will have great contentment of
*Body & Goods*.

There is the *Method* for obtaining this spirit of MERCURY, which I have revealed according to the license I was able to obtain from the sovereign Commander <note>Likely a reference to God or a high-ranking master of an initiatory order.</note>: As for my operations & *Sleights-of-Hand* <term>The French "Tours-de-Main" refers to the specific manual dexterity and secret techniques required to perform the experiment successfully.</term>, consider them well and use them wisely, so that you may avoid the pains of Hell, being faithfully warned by my admonitions.

Finally, hold it for *truth*, that without this *Spirit of* MERCURY, *GOLD cannot be made potable <term>Drinkable gold (Aurum Potabile), believed to be a universal panacea and a liquid form of the Sun's power.</term>, nor the Philosopher’s Stone accomplished*: Be content...

# ON THE SPIRIT or TINCTURE
## of VENUS <note>In the alchemical tradition, the planet Venus is always associated with the metal Copper.</note>.
### CHAPTER II.

The Planet <term>VENUS</term> <note>Copper</note> can only be calculated with great difficulty, as Mathematicians and Astronomers will admit to me; for her course is conducted differently than that of the other six planets, which is why her birth is also of another kind. I shall say then that the Birth of VENUS holds the first rank after *Mercury*; but although *Mercury* has this property of penetrating and causing action, he could nevertheless do nothing if *Venus* did not incite and push him in her particular operations, in which she operates with pleasure and beauty all at once. I do not boast here of being an Astronomer, nor of being able to calculate the Course of the...

*The Root of the sulfur of the Philosophers, which is a Celestial Spirit, as well as the Root or Origin of this spiritual and supernatural spirit of Mercury, and even the beginning or source of the spiritual Salt, is in one single thing, and is found in one and the same matter, from which the Philosopher's Stone is made and not from several things;* although the Philosophers allege **MERCURY** by itself, **SULFUR** by itself, and **SALT** by itself <note>original: "le MERCURE par soy, le SOUFRE par soy, & le SEL par soy." These are the Tria Prima, the three prime principles of all matter according to Paracelsian alchemy.</note>: but I say that by this they mean the impurities that are found within each of them. One can however make by several ways a particular Medicine for a mediocre and limited transmutation of Metals.

But this Medicine, or Universal Transmutation, which is the great Treasure of earthly Wisdom <note>original: "Sapience terrestre."</note>, made of the three Principles, is found and drawn only from a single and unique Matter, which reduces all Metals to one Principle, and first Matter, and is the true spirit of Mercury; the Soul of Sulfur; and the spiritual Body of Salt, united and enclosed corporally and spiritually together within a Celestial Matrix <note>A "Matrix" in alchemy refers to the environment or vessel—often compared to a womb—where the transformation occurs.</note> and of the same nature as them, and is the Dragon and the Eagle; the King and the Lion; the Spirit and the Body; which Medicine tints the Body of the Sun <note>The "Sun" (Soleil) is the alchemical term for Gold.</note> with a Tincture <note>A "Tincture" is the concentrated essence or "soul" of a substance capable of transforming other bodies.</note> so exuberant, and powerful, so abundant; that it has a present virtue, of tinting and fixing its companions perfectly.

For it is within this world that the **Planets** and the **Stars** dwell and reign, and where they have their course, *force, and virtue*, fulfilling their duty according to the decree of *Divine Providence*, thus causing the generation of **Metals** and **Minerals** through their spiritual influences.

The third is the **Elementary** or *Typical World*, in which are all the Elements and sublunary creatures; among which are the **Metals** and **Minerals** that derive their origin from the *spiritual influences of these first two Worlds, which incessantly imprint their virtues into this Elementary World.*

**It is** from the **Super-celestial** world that the source of **Life** and the **Soul** of all things draws its origin; and from the **Celestial** *World* comes the *light* of the **Spirit**. But it is from the third, namely the *Elementary World*, that the *imperceptible, entirely divine, and invincible* **Fire** proceeds, by which palpable things of *corporeal* solidity are refined <note>original: "decuites" - a term from "décuire," referring to alchemical maturation or "digestion" by heat.</note>. Good <term>JUPITER</term> <note>The alchemical name for the metal Tin.</note>; among all the *Metals*, it is almost the one that holds the *Middle* in its interior: For it is neither too *Hot*, nor too *Cold*; neither too *Dry*, nor too *Moist* <note>These are the four basic qualities of matter in Aristotelian physics, which alchemists sought to balance to achieve perfection.</note>. No doubt my persecutors and those who are ignorant *Physicians* will say to me what is commonly said in the proverb: *You tell me much concerning Geese, and yet you do not yet know Ducks.* <note>This likely refers to a contemporary proverb about someone who claims to understand grand or complex things (Geese) while lacking knowledge of the basic or common elements (Ducks).</note> Who is it that will assure us that everything you have written to us is true? ### ENIGMA. <note>original: "ENIGME." In alchemical literature, an enigma is a symbolic riddle used to hide the practical steps of the "Great Work" from the uninitiated while revealing them to experienced practitioners.</note>

In my dominion, I have claimed for myself from among the twelve Signs, *Sagittarius* and *Pisces*: I was born from *Pisces* <note>original: "Poiffon." The zodiac sign of the Fish, associated with the element of Water.</note>, because I was <term>Water</term> <note>original: "Eav." In alchemy, "Water" often refers to the primordial mercurial substance.</note> before my <term>Life</term>: but *Sagittarius* [the Archer] has struck my heart with his Arrow <note>original: "Sagette," an archaic French term for an arrow.</note>, by means of which I lost my *aquosity* <note>The state of being liquid or watery.</note>, having become by means of *Heat*, a *dry Earth*; and although my *Earth*, by means of *Water*, has become a *soft substance*, nevertheless you must understand that the *Water* was dried by the *hot Air*, and that this soft Matter was changed by Heat into a *hard Matter*.

Concerning this, you who are learned, *or you others who wish to learn*, you must diligently observe and take care that <term>Tin</term> <note>original: "Estain." Associated with the planet Jupiter in the alchemical system.</note> is subject to the four Elements and to the other Planets, which Elements have received into their center the virtues from above, and are engendered by them.

To bid you *adieu* [farewell], I tell you that when you extract from this good <term>Jupiter</term> <note>The alchemical name for Tin.</note> the <term>Salt</term> and the <term>Sulfur</term>; and when you join them to <term>Saturn</term> <note>The alchemical name for Lead.</note> to make them flow together, you will see <term>Saturn</term> take on a more fixed Body, purging itself and becoming clearer, and you will have a *true Transmutation* of <term>Saturn</term> into <term>Jupiter</term>.

that <term>Saturn</term> <note>The alchemical name for the metal Lead.</note> was by <term>Vulcan</term> <note>The Roman god of fire and smithing; here, it represents the action of fire upon metals.</note> rendered the least perfect of all its Companions: For the superior light has caused and engendered *Saturn* with a non-fixed body, open with many pores, so that the Air might penetrate its Body, and lift it up: but because this *Metal* is not fixed, nor very compact, the <term>FIRE</term> can easily act upon it to melt it, which he who seeks the *mysteries* of Nature within it must well observe: For there is a great difference between fixed and non-fixed bodies; and between the causes from which their permanence and their volatility proceed. And although the senses recognize *Saturn* as being heavier than some other *Metals*, Note however that when one melts it with others, notwithstanding the union given to them by the melt, the other *Metals* go to the bottom, as is seen in <term>ANTIMONY</term> when it is melted with other *Metals*; from which one learns that all others are of a more compact and tightened consistency than <term>GOOD SATURN</term>: For it must yield and give place to the others, and cannot gain any victory over them, being consumed with the volatile parts, because its *three principles* <note>The "Three Principles" (Tria Prima) are Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—the basic building blocks of all matter in alchemical theory.</note> are burdened with impurity: and because its <term>SALT</term> is more fusible than any <term>SALT</term> of the others, its Body also is more fusible and less fixed.

But so that you may learn the generation of <term>Saturn</term> <note>The alchemical name for lead.</note>; know that just as common <term>Water</term> becomes *Ice* through the coagulation caused by natural coldness proceeding from the influence of the upper heavens: likewise, one can say that <term>Saturn</term> is coagulated and given a physical body by the great coldness found in its <term>Salt</term>. And just as *Ice* is dissolved by heat, so <term>Saturn</term>, being similarly coagulated and made into *Metal*, is rendered fusible by a fire similar to that of Mount *Aetna* <note>A reference to intense, volcanic heat.</note>. There is found within it a great quantity of <term>Mercury</term> <note>One of the Three Principles; it represents liquidity and volatility.</note>, but it is not permanent, rather it is volatile; and there is a very small quantity of <term>Sulfur</term> <note>The principle of combustibility and "fire" within a metal.</note>, which is the reason why it could not be sufficiently heated. The <term>Salt</term> <note>The principle of stability and body.</note> is likewise found in small quantity, yet it is fusible, although the fusibility of <term>Saturn</term> does not proceed from the <term>Salt</term>: for if <term>Salt</term> provided fusibility and malleability, it would follow that <term>Mars</term> <note>The alchemical name for iron.</note> would be more fusible and malleable than *Saturn*. But since there are distinctions and differences among *Metals*, you must take great care in how they must be distinguished and understood.

All the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>A term for alchemists and seekers of natural wisdom.</note> have written alongside me that <term>Salt</term> provides *Coagulation* and gives physical form to every *Metal*, and it is truly so: but I shall clearly prove by an example how one [can]...

A noble Pole, endowed with no less learning than genius, anonymous, whose name was nevertheless revealed by a double anagram <margin>namely Michael Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636) was a Polish alchemist and diplomat. <meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of alchemical language, specifically addressing why the "Philosophers" (alchemists) use such obscure and ambiguous terminology. It transitions from the bibliography of authors mentioned on the previous page to a warning about the intentional difficulty of their writings.</meta>

It may be accused of ambiguity or falsehood, since nearly the same things can be expressed in many languages, through various enunciations and with a diverse array of characters; moreover, the same manifold subject can be discussed for many different reasons. 

<margin>15</margin> Take care, studious reader, regarding the multiple meanings of words; for their discourses are wreathed in deceitful twists—often even appearing contradictory—as if their mysteries were intended to be explained, entangled, and hidden all at once, not to corrupt or destroy the truth, but out of a zeal for it. For this reason, the writings of the Philosophers <note>In alchemical texts, "Philosophers" refers specifically to those who have mastered the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> abound with ambiguous terms and homonyms <note>original: "homonymia," referring to puns or words that sound the same but have different meanings.</note>; yet in nothing do they labor more intensely than in the weaving of their golden thread <note>original: "stamine ipsorum aureo." This refers to the consistent logic or "clue" that runs through their complex allegories.</note>.

> No matter how much every shadow
> Encloses the hidden valleys with obscure light,
> It does not yield to force; rather, it follows easily
> One who is willing, and who
> Recognizes the birds of his mother,
> For whom by chance the twin doves
> Descend from the sky, flying before the man's very face.
<note>This is a poetic paraphrase of Virgil's *Aeneid* (Book VI), describing Aeneas finding the Golden Bough. In alchemy, the Golden Bough represents the "matter" of the Stone, which cannot be found by brute force, but only by those favored by Nature (symbolized by the doves of Venus).</note>

Whoever seeks a certain method of perfecting and multiplying metals outside of the very nature of metals is blinded by folly. For metallic things come from metals, just as human things come from a human, and from an ox <note>The text cuts off here, but implies "comes another ox," illustrating the alchemical principle that "like produces like."</note>

17 It must be admitted that metals cannot be multiplied <note>In alchemy, "multiplication" refers to the process of increasing the quantity of gold or, more often, increasing the potency and power of the Philosopher’s Stone.</note> by the instinct and labor of nature alone; however, one may affirm that the power of multiplying is hidden in the depths of the house <note>original: "domus," often an alchemical metaphor for the internal structure of matter or the laboratory vessel itself.</note>, and that this active work is made manifest. In this work, nature requires active assistance, and only then is the entire work completed.

In this context, these are the noble metals, specifically gold and silver, which were believed to have reached a state of natural perfection.</note> are endowed with a more perfect seed. 19 Most philosophers assert that their royal work is composed entirely of the sun and the moon <note>Sun and Moon are the common alchemical symbols for Gold and Silver.</note>. Others were pleased to add Mercury to the sun; some chose sulfur and Mercury. Several do not fear to attribute a part of the work to a "salt of nature" <note>original: "sali naã" (sali naturae). Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury were the three primary components (Tria Prima) in Paracelsian alchemy.</note> mixed with those two. They have professed to produce their stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone.</note> from only one thing, sometimes from two, sometimes from three, sometimes from four, and even from five. Although they write with such a varied and learned style, they nonetheless agree in their authority.

20 Let us proceed candidly and in good faith, setting aside all deceptions. We maintain that the entire work is completed using only two bodies—namely, the **Sun** <note>original: "Solis." In alchemy, the Sun represents Gold and the masculine principle of heat and sulfur.</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: "Lunae." The Moon represents Silver and the feminine principle of moisture and mercury.</note>—when they are properly prepared. For this is the true generation which occurs through nature with art serving as its handmaid. Here, a union of the male and female takes place, from which an offspring far more noble than the parents is produced.

21 However, it is necessary that those bodies be taken in their pure and undefiled virginity, living and animated. Those bodies are called "corrupt" if they have already undergone a union, while "dead" refers to those that have poured out their soul and blood in martyrdom, having been consumed by the "supreme tyrant of the world" <note>A metaphorical reference to the harsh refining processes of the furnace or common smelting, which "kill" the metal's internal life.</note>. Flee the fratricide! <note>original: "fuge fratricidam." A traditional alchemical warning against a process that destroys the essential "living" components of the matter.</note> Recognize that the greatest danger looms throughout the entire work.

22 The **SUN** <term>Sol: The active, masculine principle in alchemy, associated with heat, the color red, and the metal Gold.</term> is the male; indeed, he is the active one and emits the formative seed. The **MOON** <term>Luna: The receptive, feminine principle, associated with coolness, the color white, and the metal Silver.</term> is the female, who is called the "matrix" or womb and the vessel of nature, because she receives the male's seed in her womb and nourishes it with her own *menstruum* <note>original: "menstruo." In the laboratory, this refers to a solvent or "mercurial water" that dissolves and feeds the solid metal seed.</note>. Yet she is not entirely lacking in active power; for she, first raging with love, climbs upon the male until she draws out from him the finest delights and fertile [seed]...

<summary>The author explains that the Great Work requires only two "living" substances, the Sun (gold) and the Moon (silver), which must be joined in a symbolic marriage. He warns the reader to avoid common, "dead" metals that have been ruined by ordinary refining processes.</summary>

By the strong and inseparable contact of pure **sulfur** <note>original: "sulfuris puri." In alchemical theory, sulfur represents the active, masculine principle that gives metals their form and color.</note>.
With which **mercury** <note>original: "mercurio." The fluid, feminine principle of matter.</note> proceeds, and so far toward that end
by being most excellently prepared, the art denies 
to anyone the means of separating them; for if any 
**permanent liquid** <term>original: "liquoris permanentis." A term for a substance that can withstand the heat of fire without evaporating.</term>
is drawn away from the base by the violence of **Fire** with water,
it should be regarded as a portion of the dissolved 
body, not a separation of the **tincture** <note>original: "tincturæ." The concentrated essence used to "dye" or transform base metals into gold.</note>.
For the tincture follows its own body and 
is in no way separated from it; that is a tight fusion 
unknown to the craftsmen themselves.

§ 2 But while it may be granted that the Tincture is separable 
from its body, it must nonetheless be admitted that it 
cannot be separated without the **corruption** <note>original: "corruptionem." This refers to the chemical breakdown or "death" of a substance's original properties.</note>
of both the body and the tincture. Since they are 
destroyed by the fire of **fusion** <note>original: "fusionis." The process of melting metals together.</note>, 
and by **strong waters** <term>original: "aquas fortes." Nitric acid or other powerful acids used to dissolve metals.</term> they are 
rendered [useless] rather than being dissolved from the gold; 
hence, the artist falls into a trap. 
The body is thus stripped of its tincture and its gold; 
it becomes utterly worthless and, like a useless weight, 
turns into a loss for its creator. There is 
no need to expect help from a corrupted tincture.

§ 3 Finally, should they cast their tincture into **mercury**
or into any other **imperfect body** <note>original: "corpus imperfectum." Any metal other than gold or silver, such as lead, tin, or copper.</note>, 
and judge strictly by the strength and skill of their art, 
they will find their hope fails in two ways: first, because the 
strength and weight of the tincture will neither penetrate 
nor **transform** <note>original: "tinget." Literally "to tinge" or "to dye," referring to the alchemical goal of changing a metal's color and nature.</note>; 
therefore, no profit will come from it 
to recover the expenses, and there will only be the 
loss of the stripped and discarded body.

<summary>This page warns that the "tincture" or transformative essence of gold is naturally inseparable from its physical body; attempting to force a separation using fire or acid usually destroys both, resulting in a useless substance and financial loss.</summary>

Ultimately, that **tincture** <note>original: "tinctura." 35. In our work, more labor and time are spent than expense; for to the one who has a **skillful hand** <note>original: "Convertentem Manum Habenti." Therefore, let the too-credulous beginner—who has prepared a great store of money and strives for a difficult task through costs rather than trusting in his own art—beware of those fierce predators. The mythical object sought by Jason and the Argonauts, used here as a metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, are tossed between the dangerous rocks of **sulfur** and **mercury**. The more perceptive seekers, through diligent practice and the reading of trustworthy authors, have gained knowledge of the sulfur from the **radiating sun** <note>original: "sole irradiante." In alchemical symbolism, the Sun represents Gold or the masculine, sulfurous principle of the work.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of the various states of "Philosophical Mercury." Having previously discussed the volatile and fixed nature of the substance, the author now focuses on the Elixir—the "fixed" state—and its relationship to the celestial planets and mythological symbols of labor.</meta>

It is improperly named, for the name of **Mercury** <term>In alchemy, Mercury usually represents the principle of volatility and fluidity. The author is clarifying that "Mercury" is often a shorthand for different stages of the material.</term> properly belongs only to a volatile thing, because it is **sublimated** in every part of the work’s governance; however, the **Elixir**, because it is most fixed, avoids the simple name of Mercury. Therefore, the Philosophers called it "their Mercury" <note>original: "suum mercurium"</note> to distinguish it from volatile substances; only there is the entire path of investigating and discerning the various Mercuries opened.

->Which the sick man loved<- <note>original: "quod aegrus amavit"; this likely refers to the "sick" or "imperfect" metals that "love" (are attracted to) the healing power of the Elixir, or perhaps a reference to the alchemist seeking a cure for mortality.</note>

**Jupiter**, when burning virtue raised him to the heavens. <note>original: "Jupiter, cum ardens evexit ad aethera virtus"; a poetic reference to the process of purification where the material is "raised" to a perfected, celestial state.</note>

41. The **Elixir** is called "spermatic mercury" <term>original: "mercurius spermus"; "spermatic" here refers to the generative "seed" or life-force within the metal that allows it to grow, multiply, or transform other substances.</term> because of its similarity and the great conformity it has with **celestial Mercury** <note>The planet Mercury.</note>. For this mercury fulfills the eternity of qualities; it is rendered more prone to sustaining them and immediately, boldly takes on the versatile nature and spirit <note>original: "genium"</note> of the other planets, and increases the state of opposition, conjunction, and aspect <note>These are astrological terms describing the positions of planets relative to one another, which alchemists believed influenced the qualities of metals and the timing of their experiments.</note>. Thus works the **Gold-bearing** <term>original: "Aurifer"; literally "gold-bringing" or "gold-producing."</term> **Elixir**: it is not tied to any single quality of its own, but whatever it is mixed with, it embraces that quality and spirit, and multiplies its virtues and qualities in a marvelous way.

42. In that **sublimation** or first preparation of Mercury, a **Herculean labor** <term>A reference to the twelve impossible tasks of Hercules, used by alchemists to describe the grueling, repetitive, and difficult physical labor required in the laboratory.</term> falls upon the operator; for without **Alcides** <note>Another name for Hercules, derived from his grandfather Alcaeus.</note> and the unencumbered, **Jason** would have attempted the Colchian expedition <note>The myth of Jason and the Argonauts seeking the Golden Fleece in Colchis was one of the most popular alchemical allegories; the "Fleece" represented the Philosopher's Stone.</note> in vain.

<summary>The author distinguishes the fixed Elixir from volatile Mercury, describing the Elixir's ability to adopt and multiply the qualities of other "planets" (metals). He emphasizes that the first preparation of this substance requires extreme effort, likened to the mythological labors of Hercules and Jason.</summary>

**§ 7.** The Philosophers plunged their Mercury more deeply into the darkness, in many ways with a covenant and [shorthand] in every part of the work and its governance; they [describe] their **Mercury** in various <unclear>ty</unclear> states, and he who is ignorant of any part of the work will not achieve a perfect knowledge of it.

**40.** Finally, it is permitted [to call] the perfected **Elixir** and the **tinging medicine** <term>A substance capable of "tinging" or transforming base metals into gold.</term> the Mercury of the Philosophers <note>original: "mercuriu suum... phi"</note>, although...

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of "Philosophical Mercury," moving from the volatile preparations to the fixed state known as the Elixir. It is improperly named, for the name of mercury properly belongs only to a volatile thing <note>original: "rei volatili"</note>, because it is sublimated <term>the process of heating a substance until it turns to vapor and then becomes solid again, used here to describe the refinement of the spirit</term> in any stage of the work’s **regimen** <term>the governed sequence of operations</term>. But the **Elixir** <term>the perfected substance or Philosopher's Stone</term>, because it is most fixed and simple, avoids the name of Mercury. Therefore, they called it "their" mercury to distinguish it from the volatile kind. A different, correct way of investigating and discerning these mercuries is woven in here.

->that which Algirus loved<- <note>original: "quod algirus amavit". "Algirus" likely refers to a specific mountain or perhaps a coded reference to a person or place in alchemical allegory.</note>

**Jupiter**, when burning **Virtue** rises to the heavens. <note>original: "Jupiter, cum ardens surgit ad aethera Virtus." This poetic line links the chemical ascent to the planetary power of Jupiter.</note>

41. The **Elixir** is called the mercury of the **sphere** <term>the celestial or elemental domain</term> because of the resemblance and the great conformity it shares with the celestial mercury. For this [substance] fills the eternity of qualities; it is believed to be more prone to sustaining them, and it assumes the versatile nature and character of the other planets for its own purpose. It increases this through its **state** of opposition, conjunction, and aspect <note>terms from astrology describing the relative positions of planets which influence their power</note>. Thus works the gold-bearing **Elixir**: since it is devoted to no quality of its own, it embraces the quality and character of whatever thing it is mixed with, and it multiplies the virtue and qualities of that thing in a wonderful way.

42. In the **sphere** of Mercury, if the labor is performed through sublimation without the first preparation, it results in a hopeless failure for the worker. For without **Alcides** <note>original: "Alcido", referring to Hercules (Alcides). In myth, Hercules accompanied Jason; in alchemy, he represents the strength or "fixed" force required for the work.</note>, **Jason** would have attempted the expedition to **Colchis** <note>the destination of the Argonauts to find the Golden Fleece, a common metaphor for the Alchemical Stone</note> in vain.

<summary>This page explains that while the final "Elixir" is often called Mercury, it is actually a "fixed" substance that takes on and multiplies the qualities of whatever it touches. It uses the myth of Jason and Hercules to warn that the Elixir cannot be achieved without proper initial preparation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of "Philosophical Mercury," following the discussion of the "Whiteness" and the astrological timing of the work. It is proclaimed, sometimes called earth, sometimes water — It is often said, also because it is naturally forged from <term>Stagnon</term> <note>original: "Stagnon." A "pistorius" is a baker; here referring to the chemical "cooking" in the furnace. ### 47
**Quicksilver** <note>original: "Argentum vivum."</note> is partly natural and partly yearly; it possesses an intrinsic and hidden quality in its naked root, which cannot be summoned except through preceding cleansings and ingenious sublimations. The extrinsic is foreign and accidental; therefore, separate the pure from the impure, the substance from the accidents <note>The author uses the Aristotelian distinction between "substance" (the essence) and "accidents" (temporary or external qualities like color or shape).</note>, and make the hidden manifest through the way of nature according to these steps, for this is the foundation of the work of the entire art.

### 48
That dry and most precious liquor constitutes the **radical moisture** <note>original: "humidum radicale." In alchemy, this is the essential life-fluid of matter that allows it to be malleable and alive.</note> of metals — for this reason, it was called "glass" by some of the ancients; for it is called glass because of the radical moisture stubbornly clinging to its bounds, which <unclear>I have seen</unclear> only from the eternal flame, **Mercury** <note>original: "mercurius."</note>.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Philosophical Mercury" or "Quicksilver," moving from its definition as a hermaphroditic substance to the practical and allegorical methods of its purification. However, our Central [substance] reveals itself, albeit through a most gentle, yet rather long, struggle.

### 49
Some work through <term>calcinations</term> <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter or cause oxidation.</note>, others through <term>sublimations</term> <note>A process where a solid is heated until it turns into a vapor and then reverts to a solid form upon cooling, used here to purify the substance.</note>. Some stir the hidden earths of those who seek among glazed vessels; certain others work among <term>vitriol</term> <note>A sulfate mineral, typically green or blue, highly valued by alchemists as a potent solvent.</note> and salt, as if among the very vessels of their birth. Others have commanded to sublime from lime and glass. 

We, however, have learned from the prophet <note>Refers to Moses, traditionally considered the author of Genesis.</note> that *In the beginning God created the Heaven and the earth; the earth was void and empty, and darkness was over the face of the abyss, and the Spirit of God moved over the waters; and God said "Let there be light," and there was light, and God saw the light that it was good, and he divided the light from the darkness,* etc. <note>original: "In principio Creavit Deus Cœlum et terram..." This is a direct citation of Genesis 1:1-4.</note>

The blessing of Joseph, announced by the same prophet, will be enough for the wise man: *From the blessing of the Lord is his land, from the fruits of Heaven and the dew and the abyss lying beneath, from the fruits of the produce of the sun and the moon, from the summit of the Ancient Mountains, from the fruits of the eternal hills,* etc. <note>original: "De benedictione Domini terra eius..." A reference to Deuteronomy 33:13-15, which alchemists interpreted as a physical description of the materials needed for the Great Work.</note> Likewise, ask from your innermost heart, my son, that He might grant you a portion of this blessed earth.

### 50
**Quicksilver** <note>original: "Argentum vivum."</note> is so defiled by <term>original sin</term> <note>An alchemical metaphor for the natural impurities found in raw minerals, which the alchemist must "redeem" through purification.</note> that it suffers from a twofold primary affliction. The first is like an unclean earth through contagion, which mixed itself in during the metal's generation and adhered through coagulation. The other is bordering on <term>dropsy</term> <note>original: "hydropisi." Historically, a swelling caused by excess fluid; here used to describe an excess of "crude" or "immature" moisture within the mercury.</note>; the water is inwardly corrupted by a thick water and

<meta>This page continues the technical and allegorical instructions for purifying the "Philosophical Mercury." Impure mixtures proceed forth, which are suppressed and separated through the <term>Constitution</term> <note>The specific structural arrangement or governing law of the alchemical process.</note>, for it was not possible [to do otherwise], and because the earth must be dried out—under heat, the body of <term>Mercury</term> <note>Quicksilver; the fluid, feminine, and volatile principle in alchemy.</note> flees above those things. The attached impurities are not of the root or substance but are accidental; therefore, they are separable from it.

Add [this] by means of a <term>moist bath</term> <note>A water bath or "bain-marie," used for gentle, indirect heating.</note> and washing, for it is cleansed; by working through a <term>dry bath</term> <note>A sand or ash bath, providing a more intense but still regulated heat.</note> with a gentle, graduated fire, it is put to flight. Thus, through persistent washings and purifications, stripped of its ancient scales and squalid skin, it is renewed.

If the Alchemy of Mercury is perfected by two things—by removing the superfluous from it through <term>sublimation</term> <note>The process of turning a solid into vapor and back to solid to purify it.</note> and by introducing what is lacking—the superfluous things are external accidents which, like the dark sphere of <term>Saturn</term> <note>The alchemical name for lead, representing heaviness, darkness, and the initial, unrefined stage of matter.</note>, cloud the glowing <term>Jupiter</term> <note>The alchemical name for tin, representing a brighter, more refined stage than lead.</note>. Therefore, separate the emerging lividness of Saturn until the stars of bright Jupiter appear to you; add <term>sulfur</term> <note>The active, masculine, and combustible principle in the alchemical Great Work.</note>—

for the sake of which it indeed possesses enough fermented Mercury within itself as suffices for its own needs; but act so that it may also suffice for others, and multiply it. Therefore, let that invisible white sulfur be brought down, so that the <term>virgin’s milk</term> <note>original: "lac virginitatis." A symbolic term for a white liquid or mercurial water used to "nourish" the philosophical stone.</note> may come to you—

->Father Janua<- <note>original: "pater Janua." Likely a reference to Janus, the Roman god of gates and transitions, symbolizing the entry into the work, or a specific alchemical authority.</note>

<summary>This page describes the purification of Mercury by stripping away "accidental" impurities (represented by Saturn) to reveal a brighter state (Jupiter) through controlled heating and "baths."
