<meta>This page shifts from the technical descriptions of "dry" and "moist" baths on the previous page to highly allegorical imagery. It describes the entrance into the symbolic "Garden of Alchemy," moving from the purification of the "Dragon" (Mercury) to the cultivation of the three philosophical flowers that represent the progression of the Great Work.</meta>

52. Entrance to the Garden of <term>Alchemy</term> <note>original: "Alchymiæ"</note>, and the Skill required —
The Guardian is the <term>Dragon</term> <note>original: "Draco"; a common symbol for the raw, volatile, and "poisonous" initial state of the matter (mercury) that must be tamed.</note>, who, once satisfied, allows a fountain of the clearest water to burst forth from a sevenfold spring. At the threshold, it reveals itself on all sides; from this, let the Dragon drink the magic number of three-times-seven <note>Twenty-one; likely referring to twenty-one cycles of distillation or purification.</note>, and may it please the Dragon until, upon meeting you, it casts off its squalid fire. Let the green <term>Lucifer</term> <note>The "Light-Bringer" or morning star (Venus), often associated in alchemy with the "Green Lion" or the first sign of life in the matter.</note> and the horned <term>Diana</term> <note>The goddess of the moon, representing the "White" or silver stage of the process, known as the Albedo.</note> be propitious deities.

53. The Three Flowers of the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophorum"</note> in the Garden —
The types of wisdom to be sought out must be discovered: the crimson <term>Violet</term>, the shining <term>Lily</term>, and the purple, immortal <term>Amaranth</term> <note>These three flowers represent the successive color changes in the alchemical vessel: the Violet (the transition to the blue/dark stage), the Lily (the White stage), and the Amaranth (the final Red stage or Rubedo).</note>, not far from that fountain. Blooming at the threshold, the Violets will first meet you; irrigated by the great golden river through small streams, they take on the most brilliant color of a dark sapphire. The Sun will give you the signs. You shall not separate these precious flowers from their root until you compose the <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the work.</note>, for they seem to hold more juice and <term>tincture</term> <note>The soul or essence of the metal that allows for transmutation.</note> when plucked; then, pluck them with a diligent, subtle, and ingenious hand. For if the fates permit, they follow easily, and when one flower is plucked, another golden one is not lacking <note>A classic reference to the Golden Bough from Virgil's "Aeneid," implying that the matter is inexhaustible and the process is divinely favored.</note>; with longer care and labor, the Lily and the Amaranth shall follow.

<summary>This page describes the transition from raw matter to the "philosophical flowers" within a symbolic garden, detailing the taming of the Dragon and the harvesting of the Violet, Lily, and Amaranth as markers of alchemical progress.</summary>
<keywords>Garden of Alchemy, Dragon, Sevenfold Spring, Diana, Lucifer, Alchemical Flowers, Violet, Lily, Amaranth, Philosopher's Stone</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical description of the alchemical process, shifting from the "Garden" of the previous page to the "Sea" and "Mountains" where the raw materials for the Philosopher's Stone are found. It focuses on the dual nature of the substances required for the work.</meta>

54. The philosophers assert that their sea also contains a pool of fatty spring-water <note>original: "stagnu[m] pinguis scaturiginis"; this "fatty" or oily substance refers to the "Oil of the Sages," a stage in the purification of the matter</note>. They marvel at the little fish found within it; he who entangles them in a fine net and draws them out must be considered a noble and most skillful fisherman.

55. The **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: "Lapis p[hilosophorum]"</note> is found in the most ancient mountains and is dug out from similar excellent excavations. Those mountains are made of silver and, likewise, of gold. From there, treasures of gold and silver of every kind are produced.

56. Whoever wishes to compose the **Philosopher's Stone** must undertake long pilgrimages and travel across the "five Indias" <note>A metaphorical reference to the vast and exotic spiritual or physical journey required to find the true hidden ingredients</note>; it is necessary to bring back from there the whitest gems and the purest gold.

57. The philosophers extract their Stone from seven stones, two of which are especially of a different nature and virtue. One provides the physical substance <note>original: "substantia"</note>, while the other infuses <term>spiritual mercury</term> <note>original: "mercurius spiritualis"; a refined, volatile essence of the matter</note>. The former provides heat and dryness, while the latter induces coldness and moisture <note>These represent the four Aristotelian qualities which must be balanced: Hot/Dry (Fire/Sulfur) and Cold/Moist (Water/Mercury)</note>. Thus, at last, the powers of the operator are multiplied within the stone. The first is found in the eastern region, the second in the western. Both have the power of <term>tincture</term> <note>The power to transform the color and quality of base metals</note> and <term>multiplication</term> <note>The power of the Stone to increase its own potency or the amount of gold it can produce</note>; unless the Stone draws its coloring from them, it will neither transform nor multiply.

<summary>This page describes the symbolic locations (the fatty sea and the gold/silver mountains) where alchemical materials are found and details the dual nature of the Stone, requiring a balance of "hot/dry" and "cold/moist" essences.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Spiritual Mercury, Tincture, Multiplication, Alchemical Elements, Five Indias, Symbolic Geography</keywords>

--- Page 162 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructional sequence from the previous page, moving from the general description of the "two stones" (mercury and sulfur) to specific "practices" or allegorical recipes for the creation of the Philosopher's Stone. It describes the union of opposites through the imagery of a winged virgin and the battle between an eagle and a lion.</meta>

<margin>practice</margin>
58. Take the **winged Virgin** <term>winged Virgin</term> <note>original: "Virginem alatam"; in alchemy, "wings" signify volatility. The winged virgin is a common symbol for Mercury, the fluid, changing element of the work</note>, very well washed and cleansed. Impregnated with the seed of the **first male** <note>The "male" refers to the fixed, sulfurous principle that acts upon the mercury</note>, she will be kept pregnant while the glory of her unsullied virginity is preserved. Tinged with a **reddish-purple color** <note>original: "puniceo Colore"; this refers to the "Rubedo" or reddening stage, the final and highest state of the alchemical process</note>, she will bring forth her kind. Join her to a **second male** without any suspicion of adultery; from his body she will conceive seed again, and at last she will give birth to a venerable offspring of both sexes. From this, an immortal lineage of most powerful Kings shall arise.

<margin>practice 2.</margin>
59. By placing both the **Eagle** <note>Another symbol for volatile Mercury</note> and the **Lion** <note>The "fixed" or solid principle, often associated with Gold or Antimony</note>, each perfectly purified, into their **cloister** <note>original: "In Claustro suo"; the sealed glass vessel or furnace where the reaction takes place</note>, they must be joined in union. The entrance must be most strictly closed and guarded, lest their breath escape or external air creep in. In their encounter, the **Eagle** will tear apart and devour the **Lion**. Then, seized by a long sleep and with a belly swollen with fluid <note>original: "Hydrops facta"; literally "becoming dropsical." This describes the stage where the solids dissolve into a liquid state</note>, it will pass through a wonderful **metamorphosis** into a **jet-black Raven** <note>original: "Corvum nigerrimum"; this represents the "Nigredo" or black stage, a period of putrefaction and darkness necessary before purification</note>. This Raven, gradually unfolding its wings, will begin to fly; in its flight, it will bring down water from the clouds until, having been soaked many times, it sheds its feathers of its own accord. Falling back down, it transforms into the **whitest wood** <note>original: "Lignum Migret Candidissimum"; a reference to the "Albedo" or white stage, signifying the purification of the matter into the "White Stone"</note>. Let those who are ignorant of the causes of things marvel in astonishment. Considering that the world is nothing other than a continuous metamorphosis, let them marvel perfectly.

<summary>This page provides two allegorical "practices" for the alchemical work, using symbolic figures like the winged virgin, the eagle, and the lion to describe the chemical union and transformation of substances. It details the transition through the major alchemical stages of blackness (Nigredo), whiteness (Albedo), and redness (Rubedo).</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the alchemical process, moving from the definition of "remedies" on the previous page to the specific sequence of operations: the symbolic marriage, the separation of elements, the purification of the body, and the first major stage of the Great Work, the "Nigredo" or blackening.</meta>

The **union of the male and female** <note>original: "Coitus maris et foeminae"; a central alchemical metaphor where two opposing principles—usually Mercury and Sulfur—are combined to begin the process of creation</note> from which the body is generated; then follows the **separation of the stone** <term>separation</term> <note>original: "separatio lapidis"; the process of breaking a substance down into its constituent parts to purify them</note> into four confused **elements** <note>original: "quatuor elementa"; the classical elements of Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note>, which happens through the **retrogradation of the luminaries** <note>original: "retrogradationem luminarium"; likely referring to an astrological timing or a reversal of a chemical process, modeled on the apparent backward motion of planets</note>. Then comes the **washing of the body** <term>washing</term> <note>original: "ablutio"; also known as "purification" or "cleansing" of the material</note>, its **whitening** <term>whitening</term> <note>original: "dealbatio"; the 'Albedo' stage where the material turns from black to white</note>, and from **Saturn** comes **Jupiter** <note>original: "ex saturno jovis"; in alchemical shorthand, Saturn represents lead and Jupiter represents tin. This signifies the transition from a dull, "heavy" state to a more refined, "shining" state</note> and <unclear>the raising</unclear> <note>original: "edatio"</note>, which is done through the conversion of the body into spirit. Led by the pure <unclear>writing</unclear> <note>original: "grammato"</note> of the stone, the soul is restored; it nourishes the stone with a foul and **spiritual milk** <note>original: "spirituali lacte"; a symbolic reference to the "mercurial water" used to feed the developing "Philosophical Child" or Stone</note> until it has reached a strengthened power. In both of these latter operations, the **dragon** <note>original: "draco"; a common symbol for volatile matter or the raw material of the work</note> turns upon itself and, by **devouring its own tail** <note>original: "caudam suam devorando"; the Ouroboros, representing a self-contained, cyclical process where the substance is dissolved and reconstituted in its own "blood" or essence</note>, it exhausts itself entirely and is finally converted into a stone. At last, the operation of **fixation** <term>fixation</term> <note>original: "fixationis"; making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</note> fixes the matter and it is anointed; **sulfur** <note>original: "sulphur"; the active, masculine principle of fire and soul in alchemy</note> is fixed upon its own body by means of the spirit, providing the **tincture** <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "tincturam"; the power of the Stone to instantly transform or "dye" base metals into gold</note> through the **ferment** <note>original: "fermenta"; a substance added to the work to "leaven" it and give it the specific quality of gold or silver</note> through its various steps. Finally, it matures the raw, sweetens the bitter, and at last—flowing, penetrating, and tinting—<unclear>the spirit</unclear> <note>original: "quidat"</note> completes the work and raises it to the **highest peak of sublimity** <note>original: "sublimitatis apicem"; the completion of the Great Work</note>.

There are four intermediary demonstrative signs which, with the succession of colors in their proper order, demonstrate a great affinity and the "passions" or changes of the matter. Because the conquering force then prevails, the same <unclear>internal</unclear> <note>original: "interi"</note> things must be noted. Some add a fourth sign, but the first is **black** <note>original: "niger"</note>, which is called the **raven’s head** <note>original: "caput corvi"; the traditional name for the Nigredo stage</note> due to its extreme **blackness** <note>original: "nigredine"</note>. It is also called the finished twilight of the night, and then <unclear>death</unclear> <note>original: "nax"</note> and the blackest perfection of the **solution** <term>solution</term> <note>original: "solutionis"; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state</note> is achieved.

66. <meta>This page continues from the previous discussion of the "Four Digestions" or regimes of fire. 69. A new Heaven and a new Earth are formed because, finally, the bodies become spiritual. The young of the ravens, changed into doves <note>The raven (black) turning into the dove (white) represents the transition from the "Nigredo" or blackening stage to the "Albedo" or whitening stage</note>, begin to migrate by means of their wings; the eagle and the lion embrace in an eternal embrace <note>The eagle usually represents the volatile or gaseous substances, while the lion represents the fixed or solid substance. For the water of **Alchemy** <term>Alchemy</term> is a fiery water, which is set in motion when excited by the heat of the bath. But I see that the separation of the waters must be done in weight and measure, lest those waters which remain under the Heaven submerge the earth, or those which are snatched above the Heaven leave the land too dry.

<margin>Here it remains stable, lest moisture fail the dry land.</margin>

70. The third **digestion**, with the green earth being born, makes the work flowery and flourishing. It offers every spiritual virtue of the **quintessence** <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: "quintæ essentiæ"; the "fifth essence" or purest part of a substance, beyond the four elements</note> and vivifies the soul, binding it to the body by means of the spirit. Then the earth hides a great treasure within itself, and thus, having been consecrated, it is to be attributed first to the moon, and then to the sun.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the alchemical "Digestions." While the previous page focused on the purification of the material into the "Earth of the Sun," this section explains the mechanical union of spirit and body and draws a direct parallel between the creation of the Philosopher’s Stone and the biblical Creation of the World.</meta>

The fixation of the Body and the coagulation of the spirit are one and the same operation. For if the fixed <note>original: "Corpus"; literally "Body," referring to the solid, stable part of the chemical substance</note> and the volatile <note>original: "volatile"; the part of the substance that evaporates or escapes when heated</note> are perfectly mixed and united—which cannot happen unless the Body has first been dissolved and made volatile—the volatile is then fixed into the Body and remains there. This occurs because of the reduction of the Body. Truly, the volatile elements finally migrate into a fixed state, just as the fixed elements previously passed into a volatile state. As long as they remain confused in their nature, the mixture itself maintains a middle nature between Body and spirit, and between the fixed and the volatile.

73. The generation of the fixed Stone <note>The "Philosopher’s Stone," the goal of the alchemical work</note> follows the example of the **Creation of the World**. For it is necessary that this work has its own Chaos and a prime matter <note>original: "manam primam," likely a variation of "materiam primam"</note> in which all things fluctuate in confusion and error until they are separated by the fire-spirit. Once separated, the light parts are carried upward and the heavy parts are carried downward. Darkness is born again; then, the waters are gathered into one place and the dry land appears. At last, the two **Great Lights** <note>original: "Luminaria Magna"; in Genesis, the Sun and Moon; in alchemy, Gold and Silver or the Red and White tinctures</note> emerge one after the other, and the mineral, vegetable, and animal virtues are produced within the earth.

74. God created Adam from the mud of the earth, into whom the virtues of all the elements were implanted.

<meta>This page continues the alchemical allegory comparing the "Great Work" to the biblical Genesis. Following the discussion of the "Adamic Earth" and the infusion of the soul on the previous page, the text now details the physical process of uniting the body and spirit (fixation) and mirrors the chronological stages of Creation—from Chaos to the appearance of life.</meta>

The **coagulation of the spirit** <term>Coagulatio: The chemical or symbolic process of turning a gaseous or "spiritual" substance into a solid form</term> and the fixing of the body are one and the same operation. For if the body and the **volatile** <term>Volatile: In alchemy, a substance that easily evaporates or escapes; often associated with the "spirit"</term> are perfectly mixed and united—which cannot happen unless the body is first dissolved by the volatile—then, because of the reduction of the body, the volatile is fixed into the body and remains there. Indeed, the volatile elements finally migrate into the body, just as the fixed elements previously transitioned into a volatile state. As long as they remain mixed in their own nature, this mixture maintains a middle nature between Body and spirit, the fixed and the volatile.

73. The generation of the **Stone** <note>original: "Lapidis generatio"; referring to the Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of alchemy</note> is established according to the example of the **Creation of the World** <note>original: "Creationis Mundi"</note>. For it is necessary that this work has a **Chaos** <term>Chaos: The primordial, unformed state of matter; the "materia prima" before it is purified</term> and a first matter, in which things float in a confused and crude state until they are separated by a **fiery spirit**. Once separated, the light things are carried upward and the heavy things downward. At last, darkness arises; then the waters are gathered together into one place, and the dry land appears. Finally, the **two Great Luminaries** <note>original: "Luminaria Magna"; the Sun and the Moon, representing the masculine and feminine principles in alchemy</note> emerge in succession, and minerals, vegetables, and animals are produced upon the earth by their own inherent virtue.

74. God created **Adam** from the mud of the earth, in which the virtues of all the elements were implanted.

<meta>This page continues the technical and symbolic discussion of the "Wheels" or "Circles" of the alchemical process. ...let the process be more fully described to this end by an <unclear>independent</unclear> treatise.

84. Each **Circle** <term>Circulus: In alchemy, this refers to a repeating cycle of distillation and condensation, often called "circulation," where a substance is vaporized and condensed repeatedly to purify it.</term> has its own proper motion; however, all motions of the **Circles** are understood to occur around the underlying **Matter**, and they are so linked together that they produce a unified operation. Although they are in harmony, two of them are opposites—both in their limits and in their causes and effects. For one moves upward through **heat** by "desiring" <note>original: "desiderando"; this suggests an active, volatile attraction or rising movement toward the top of the vessel.</note>, the other moves downward through **cold** by "generating" <note>original: "generatando," likely a scribe's variation of *generando*.</note>, while a third is imagined as residing in rest and sleep, performing **digestion** <note>original: "digerendo"; the process of slow, steady maturation of the substance through gentle, consistent heat.</note>. Thus, the highest tempered balance brings about the **cooking** <term>Coctio: A central alchemical concept where heat is applied over time to "ripen" or mature the matter into a more perfect state, similar to how fruit ripens.</term> of both.

85. The first of the **Circles'** actions is **evacuation**, the labor of which lies in withdrawing superfluous moisture, as well as in separating what is pure, clean, and subtle from the thick and earthy dregs. However, the greatest danger resides in the motion of this **Circle**, because it may draw away the **spiritual** <term>Spiritualia: The volatile, refined essences or "spirits" of a substance. In the laboratory, these are the vapors that can easily be lost if the seal of the vessel is not perfect or if the heat is too high.</term> parts and <unclear>expose</unclear> the nature.

86. Two things are especially to be avoided while moving this **Circle**: first, that the intensity is not too great; and second, that it is not moved for longer than commanded, lest the motion accelerate and cause confusion within the **Matter**.

The previous circle was the organ of sweat and labor, but this one is truly that of cooling and consolation. Its action is occupied with the water and the softening of the earth, turning according to the nature of each, so that they might be better mixed.

88. <meta>This page transitions from the discussion of the "Second Circle" of the alchemical process to the "Third Circle." The two previous circles <note>original: "circuli" — stages or recurring cycles of the alchemical process</note> work especially on dissolving; this one works on congealing, even though it operates through the whole duration of days.

If the laws of this circle are that it should be moderated by a feverish and very slow heat <note>original: "calore" — heat or fire</note>, lest the volatile parts escape and be disturbed, and the spirits are bound by the strictest conjunction <note>original: "coniunctione" — the alchemical union of two opposite substances, often described as a marriage between "Body" and "Spirit"</note> with the body; then indeed the work is carried out in the greatest tranquility and repose. Therefore, rather than attempting to direct them, it is better to yield to the earth. Finally, this third circle must always follow immediately after the second and in its own order, according to the first. Thus, through the previously completed works and by moving gently, those circles of elevation complete one whole circulation; and when this path is repeated many times, it finally turns toward the earth and establishes the parts within their boundaries.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the alchemical process, shifting from the specific stages of digestion and inhumation mentioned on page 19 to a foundational theory of Fire. It defines Fire not just as a physical flame, but as a philosophical and natural force essential for the generation of all things.</meta>

92. Fire is said to be nature’s applied art <note>original: "ars adhibita"</note>, used as an example and as both instrument and hammer in forging its works; therefore, in the operations of both [nature and art], fire is the master and the one who brings things to perfection. For this reason, the knowledge of fires itself is of the highest importance; one must know how nature will work in vain if the wrong type of fire <note>original: "alter Ignis reatus" — literally a 'guilty' or 'wrongful' fire, implying an improper temperature or source</note> is bound to the labor.

93. Among the philosophers <note>original: "p̄hos" (philosophos) — in this context, the alchemists</note>, the name of Fire is a homonym <note>original: "homonymum" — a word with multiple meanings</note>, and thus when it is used metonymically <note>original: "Metonymicè" — a figure of speech where a thing is called by the name of one of its attributes, such as using 'fire' for 'heat'</note> for heat, it encompasses everything as extensive as heat itself. In the generation of metals and plants, nature recognizes a threefold Fire: namely, the **Celestial**, the **Terrestrial**, and the **Innate**. 

The first, **Celestial**, flows down from the sun as if from its own source into the womb of the earth; it governs the mercurial <term>Mercurial</term> <note>pertaining to Mercury, the alchemical principle of fluidity, transformation, and the 'spirit' of matter</note> and sulfurous <term>Sulfurous</term> <note>pertaining to Sulfur, the alchemical principle of soul, combustible heat, and 'form'</note> vapor or smoke from which metals are created. It moves the metals and mixes itself with them; it awakens the temperate **Innate** fire within the seeds of plants and adds a little spark to it, acting as a spur toward their generation. 

The second, **Terrestrial**, lies hidden in the bowels of the earth. By its impulse and action, subterranean vapors are driven upwards through pores and small tubes, extending from the center toward the surface of the earth. This occurs both for the composition of metals where the earth bulges, and for the production of plants, by rotting <note>original: "putrefaciendo" — the alchemical stage of *putrefactio*, where matter must decay to be reborn</note> and softening their seeds, preparing them for germination.

Fire, Metonymically, Celestial, Terrestrial, Innate, Mercurial, Sulfurous, Metals, Plants

94. In the work of the matter itself, three types of fire must be observed: the natural, the unnatural, and the fire against nature. They call the **natural fire** that fiery, celestial, and innate spirit which is guarded in the depths of the matter, bound by the tightest of bonds. It desires to possess strength for the sake of the metals until it is stirred by external fire and external heat; once it is set free, [it transforms] its bodies...

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the "threefold fire" in alchemical practice. It specifically clarifies the roles of "Unnatural Fire" and "Fire against Nature," explaining how they relate to the "Innate Fire" described on the previous page to facilitate the stages of corruption and generation.</meta>

...the power of moving what has been dissolved from its distractions, then by applying, penetrating, expanding, and finally coagulating its moist, unformed **matter** <term>matter</term> <note>original: "materiam" — the physical substance undergoing transformation</note>. For in every mixture, the nature of **Fire** is the principle of heat and motion.

They call the **Unnatural Fire** that which is summoned from an external aid by a wondrous artifice and is thus introduced into the matter; through this, the natural vigor is increased and multiplied. However, they name the **Fire against Nature** <note>original: "Ignem contra naturam"</note> that which causes the compound to <term>putrefy</term> <note>original: "putrefacit" — the process of breaking down matter to its primary state through rot or heat</note>. Because the temperament of <term>nature</term> <note>original: "natura"</note> has been corrupted, it is imperfect; being powerless for <term>generation</term> <note>original: "generationem" — the creation or birth of a new form</note>, it does not proceed beyond the limits of corruption. Such is the Fire or "dead heat." Nevertheless, the name "Fire against Nature" is applied to it improperly, because in a certain way it acts according to nature: as long as the **specific form** <term>specific form</term> <note>original: "forma specifica" — the essential identity of a substance</note> is preserved, it corrupts the matter in such a way as to prepare it for generation.

95. However, it is believable that this so-called "corrupting fire against nature" is not different from the **Innate Fire**, but is rather its first stage. For since the order of nature demands that <term>corruption</term> <note>original: "corruptio" — the destruction of the old form</note> precedes generation, this Innate Fire—acting in accordance with the law of nature and never opposing it—is of a twofold nature...

It argues that the fire causing rot and corruption is actually the necessary first stage of the natural fire's work to prepare matter for new life.</summary>

--- Page 184 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the "threefold fire" in alchemical practice. It specifically clarifies the roles of "Unnatural Fire" and "Fire against Nature," explaining how they relate to the "Innate Fire" described on the previous page to facilitate the stages of corruption and generation.</meta>

The movement of Fire operates successively. First, the movement of <term>Corruption</term> <note>original: "Corruptionis" — the breaking down of a substance's form to its base elements</note> and <term>Solution</term> <note>original: "Solutionis" — the process of dissolving or liquefying a solid</note> is stirred up by a weak heat to soften and prepare the **Body**. The second movement, that of <term>generation</term> <note>original: "generationis"</note> and <term>Vegetation</term> <note>original: "Vegetationis" — the alchemical growth or "life" of the matter</note>, is driven by a more forceful heat to unite the body (already prepared by the previous stage) and to animate and fully give it form. Therefore, a twofold movement is produced by two degrees of heat from the same Fire. While Fire should be considered twofold, the names "violent fire" and "destructive fire" are far more rightfully applied to the **Fire against Nature**.

96. The **Unnatural Fire** is converted into **natural** or **Innate Fire** through successive stages of digestion. As it increases and multiplies, the entire **Secret** <note>original: "Arcanum" — a hidden or mysterious power or knowledge</note> consists in this multiplication of the natural fire. In its simple state, natural fire can neither act beyond its own strength nor communicate a perfect **tincture** <note>original: "tincturam" — the transformative essence capable of changing the color and quality of metals</note> to imperfect bodies; it is sufficient only for itself and has nothing more to give. But when multiplied by the Unnatural Fire—which is most abundant in the power of multiplying—it acts far more potently and reaches beyond the boundaries of Nature, tinting and perfecting foreign and imperfect bodies because of its exuberant tincture and the consumed <term>Treasure</term> <note>original: "Thesaurum" — the concentrated storehouse of alchemical power</note> of the multiplied fire.

97. The philosophers also call their **Water** "Fire," because it is exceedingly hot and imbued with a fiery spirit; for this reason, they call it the **Water of Fire** because...

**Vocabulary:** Movement of Fire, Corruption, Solution, Generation, Vegetation, Natural Fire, Violent Fire, Secret, Tincture, Water of Fire.

<summary>This page explains how different intensities of fire drive alchemical transformation, moving from the initial breakdown of matter to the growth of a new form. It highlights the "Secret" of multiplication, where an external fire is used to empower the natural fire to transform imperfect substances.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of alchemical fire, moving from the definitions of "Unnatural" and "Innate" fire to their practical application in dissolving perfect metals and the necessity of an external heat source to "awaken" the internal life-force within the matter.</meta>

The <term>bodies of perfect metals</term> <note>original: "Corpora perfectorum metallorum" — typically referring to gold and silver, which were considered "perfected" by nature</note> are burned more quickly and more thoroughly by this fire than by common fire; for it dissolves them perfectly, even though they resist our ordinary fire and do not allow themselves to be dissolved by it. For this reason, it is also called <term>burning water</term> <note>original: "aqua ardens" — while often meaning alcohol in early chemistry, here it refers to a potent alchemical solvent or "mercurial water"</note>. However, the fire of the <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "tinctura" — the transformative essence of the Stone</note> is hidden within the "water of the heart," and it reveals itself through a twofold effect: the <term>dissolution</term> of the body and the resulting <term>multiplication</term> <note>The process of increasing the power or quantity of the alchemical medicine</note>.

98. Nature employs a twofold fire in the <term>Great Work</term> <note>original: "opere magno" — the quest to create the Philosopher's Stone</note>: an internal one and an external one. The first, which dwells with the mixed seeds of the matter, is hidden at their very center; it moves and gives life to its **body** as the principle of motion and life. The second fire, however—whether it is drawn from the heavens or revealed from the earth—awakens the first fire as if from a deep sleep and compels it to act. Indeed, the <term>vital sparks</term> <note>original: "igniculi vitales" — literally "little vital fires"</note> innate in the seeds require an external mover so that they may move and perform their work.

99. A similar situation exists in the <term>physical work</term> <note>original: "opere physico"</note>, for the **matter of the stone** possesses its own internal fire. This fire is partly innate and partly added through physical means. These two fires seem to yield to one another because they are of the same nature <note>original: "homogenei" — of the same kind</note>. But before the external fire—which is administered as a "fat" or "rich" heat according to the rules of Art and Nature—can drive the internal fire into motion, it provides that fire...

<summary>This page explains how a specialized "burning water" dissolves perfect metals like gold and details the relationship between internal "vital sparks" and the external heat required to activate them during the Great Work.</summary>

...perform their work. It is as if there are two wheels, whose hidden seeds are set in motion by the striking of the sensible [seeds]; in this way, Art assists Nature.

100. The <insert>internally</insert> moving **Fire** remains always within the matter; thus, the matter is moved by it in terms of quality, specifically according to whether the mover is more intense or more relaxed. In the same manner, the Agent will perform its work. Finally, the <term>Informing</term> <note>original: "informatio" — the process of giving form, structure, or essence to the matter</note> of the entire work depends upon the measure of the external fire.

101. He who is ignorant of the degrees and specific points of the external fire should not attempt the <term>physical work</term> <note>original: "opus physicum" — the practical laboratory operations of alchemy</note>; for the external light never operates <note>original: "dicit" — literally "speaks," likely an OCR error or idiomatic use for "acts/expresses itself"</note> except by transferring heat through its media, just as distant things are not connected to external ones except through intermediary means.

102. Because the entire work consists in the separation of the four elements and the perfect preparation of the four elements of the <insert>stone</insert> <note>original: "lapidis"</note>, just as many degrees of fire are necessary for this task; for every book <note>original: "liber" — could also refer to a "freeing" of the substance or a specific stage of the instructions</note> in observing the work is revealed by its own proper degree of fire.

103. The four degrees of fire are called: the fire of the **bath** <note>original: "balnei" — the Balneum Mariae or water bath, used for gentle heat</note>, of **ashes** <note>original: "cinerum"</note>, of **coals** <note>original: "carbonum"</note>, and of **flame**, which is also called the exterior fire. Each degree, however, has its own points—at least two, and sometimes three. For the fire must be moved gradually and by specific points, whether it is being increased or decreased, and following the example of nature, it should lead the media slowly and spontaneously toward...

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the external fire required for alchemical work. While the previous page established that fire acts as an external mover to "awaken" the matter, this section begins by emphasizing that this process must mirror the gentle, non-violent cycles of nature—specifically the sun—before categorizing the specific technical degrees of heat used in the laboratory (water-bath, ash, sand, and coal).</meta>

...revealed through shaping and completion. Let the work proceed: for nothing is so foreign to <term>nature</term> <note>original: "natura"</note> as violence. Therefore, one should consider the gentle approach and retreat of the sun as a model—the sun being the torch and lamp of the whole world which, according to the logic of times and places, graciously offers its heat and provides a balanced temperament.

104. The first stage of the heat of the bath <note>original: "balnei" — refers to the *balneum Mariae* or water-bath</note> is gradually called "feverish heat"; the second is simply the bath itself. The first stage of the second degree is the simple heat of ashes <note>original: "cinerum"</note>, while the second is the heat of sand <note>original: "arenae" — a sand-bath provided a more intense, even heat than water</note>. The specific stages of coal fire and flame fire, however, lack their own names, but are distinguished by their Intensity or Slackening depending on the goal of the operation.

105. Although sometimes only certain degrees of Fire are found in the writings of <term>Alchemists</term> <note>original: "Alchimos" — practitioners of the ancient art of transformation</note>—specifically those of the bath, the ashes, and the burning fire (which includes both coals and flame)—the fire of dung <note>original: "fimi" — the heat generated by fermenting manure, used for gentle, steady digestion</note> is sometimes categorized separately from the degree of the bath fire. Thus, through the various expressions used by authors, they wrap the clarity of the descriptions of Fire in obscure language; for this reason, the knowledge of these fires is considered among the most important secrets <note>original: "arcana"</note> of the universe.

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of alchemical fires. While the previous page focused on the physical mediums (water, ash, sand), this section links those degrees of heat to the specific stages of the transformation, specifically the "white work," and the symbolic progression of the sun and moon.</meta>

106. In the white work <note>original: "opere albo" — the stage of purification, or *albedo*, where the matter is cleansed of impurities</note>, because only three elements are extracted, three degrees of Fire are also sufficient. Our <term>reverberation</term> <note>original: "reverbero" — a type of furnace or technique where heat is reflected or "reverberated" back onto the matter</note> prevails, with the fourth of the elements being set aside, as it completes the work as one. Through the first degree, it thus receives [the light] of the sun and the moon; through the second, the light of the moon begins to be restored; through the third, the moon attains the fullness of its splendor; and through the fourth, the sun is openly exalted in its highest glory. In every part, indeed, the Fire is administered according to the rule of <unclear>geometry</unclear>. Likewise, <term>azoth</term> <note>original: "azoth" — the essential transformative agent in alchemy, often identified with Mercury or the "Universal Solvent"</note> corresponds to a disposition of patience, and finally, the forces are released among themselves at the moment of <unclear>labor</unclear>.

107. Out of a desire to conceal his Fire, Arnobius <note>A name associated with several early Christian and later alchemical writings</note> and <unclear>others</unclear> have scarcely dared to touch upon it directly, but they indicate it through descriptions of its properties and qualities rather than by its name. And thus the Fire is called vaporous, moist, and dry, a flame, and other terms; it is something which can easily be intensified or slackened step by step at the command of the craftsman. For one desiring more knowledge of the Fire, they should find satisfaction in the works of Lullius <note>original: "Lullii" — Ramon Llull, a 13th-century polymath whose name was frequently attached to alchemical "testaments"</note>, who honestly revealed the practical secrets to honest souls.

108. Various things have been written concerning the conflict between the eagle and the lion <note>The "eagle" usually represents volatile substances (those that evaporate), while the "lion" represents fixed substances (those that resist fire)</note>, because the Lion is the strongest of animals...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of alchemical processes, transitioning from the degrees of fire to the allegorical "battle" of sublimation and the specific construction of the vessels (the "Philosophical Egg" and laboratory apparatus) required for the Great Work.</meta>

Is more added to the <term>eagle</term> <note>original: "aquila" — in alchemy, the eagle typically represents the volatile spirit (mercury) which "flies up" during sublimation to "devour" or dissolve the fixed matter (the lion)</note> for the purpose of devouring it? It would not be conquered [by the eagle alone], but by fewer or even more [eagles], up to ten. The fewer there are, the harder the <term>struggle</term> <note>original: "Luta," likely a misspelling of "lucta," meaning a fight or wrestling match</note> and the slower the victory. However, the more eagles there are, the shorter the battle, and the swifter the tearing apart of the <term>Lion</term> <note>original: "Lonis," likely "Leonis" — the "Green Lion" or the fixed material being dissolved</note> follows. This is accomplished more strongly by the hands of seven eagles from <unclear>Vultio</unclear>, when the <unclear>dew</unclear> <note>original: "roudy," possibly a corruption of "rore" (dew) or a specific cryptic substance</note> is taken from the elder.

109. The **Vessel** which we require for the work of the philosophers is of two kinds: one of **nature**, the other of **art**. The **Vessel of Nature**, which is also called the "dual [nature] of philosophy," is the "earth of the stone," whether dissolved or even matured; if the <term>male gum</term> <note>original: "gummi masculi" — a reference to the active, sulfuric principle or "white gum" that seeds the work</note> is received, it putrefies and is prepared for generation. The **Vessel of Art**, however, is triple, if the threefold secret of this vessel is understood.

110. The **first vessel of art** is constructed from a transparent material or from the "glass of the stone." Some have sought to hide its form through certain enigmatic descriptions, sometimes asserting it is composed of two pieces—namely, the <term>alembic</term> <note>The "head" or top part of a distilling apparatus</note> and the <term>cucurbit</term> <note>The "gourd" or bottom vessel of a still</note>—and sometimes of three, by adding a third piece to the two mentioned above, namely the **Cover** <note>original: "Coopertorio" — a lid or seal used to close the system for circulation</note>.

<summary>This page describes the "Eagles" (the number of sublimations) required to dissolve the "Lion" (fixed matter) and defines the two types of alchemical vessels: the natural "earth" and the artificial glass apparatus.</summary>

--- Page 190 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of alchemical apparatus, moving from the symbolic "eagles" and "lions" of the previous page to a practical critique of the specialized vessels used in the laboratory. It bridges the theoretical "Vessel of Art" mentioned in section 110 with specific physical instructions for the glass "Philosophical Egg."</meta>

111. Many people imagined that a great variety of vessels was necessary for the **alchemical work** <note>original: "operi Alchico"</note>, giving them various names based on the different operations—largely to hide the <term>method</term> <note>original: "axiam," likely a corruption of "viam" (the way) or referring to the "axis" or central secret of the work</note>. They would speak of a **dissolver** <note>original: "solutorium"</note> for dissolution, a **putrefier** <note>original: "putrefactorium"</note> for putrefaction, a **distiller** <note>original: "distillatorium"</note> for distillation, a **sublimator** <note>original: "sublimatorium"</note> for sublimation, a **calciner** <note>original: "calcinatorium"</note> for calcination, and other similar names.

112. But with all deceit removed, let us act with honesty. Since the slow pace of this art is sufficient to complete the work of any <term>sulfur</term> <note>original: "sulfuris" — in alchemical theory, sulfur represents the active, masculine, and combustible principle of matter</note>, the work of the **Elixir** is another matter. The various stages of "digestion" <note>the process of heating a substance at a gentle, consistent temperature</note> do not require one to change vessels; on the contrary, the goal is better reached without changing them, as it is more beneficial for the process to remain continuous until the very end.

113. The form of the **glass vessel** should be round at the bottom, like a **gourd** <note>original: "cucurbita" — a standard alchemical flask used for heating</note>, or even oval. The neck should extend at least one <term>palm</term> <note>an ancient unit of measurement roughly the width of a human hand</note> or more; it should be wide enough, but with a narrow mouth fashioned like a flask. One should choose a vessel that is seamless, continuous, and thick throughout.

<summary>The author argues against the need for multiple specialized alchemical vessels, stating that a single, continuous process in one vessel is more effective. He provides specific instructions for the construction of a thick, gourd-shaped glass vessel with a long neck for the "work of the Elixir."</summary>

<keywords>vessels, alchemical apparatus, sulfur, elixir, digestion, cucurbit, glassmaking, laboratory practice</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of alchemical vessels from page 24. It concludes the description of the "blind gourd" and introduces the second and third essential vessels: the wooden incubator and the furnace (athanor). The text transitions from the shape of the glassware to the materials and construction of the heating apparatus.</meta>

...so that it resists the fire even when placed far from it. It is called a **blind gourd** <note>original: "Cucurbita caeca" — a flask without an opening at the top</note> because it is closed and sealed with a **Hermetic seal** <note>original: "sigillo Hermetico" — an airtight closure made by melting the glass neck of a vessel to fuse it shut</note> so that nothing from the outside can enter, nor can the internal spirit escape.

114. The **second vessel of the art** should be made of wood. You may then ask whether it should consist of two hollowed-out **hemispheres** <note>original: "Hemisphaeria"</note> in which the <term>fertile egg</term> <note>original: "ovum phorum" — a term for a fertilized egg; in alchemy, this refers to the glass vessel containing the "seed" of the Stone</note> is kept warm until it "hatches" <note>original: "pullificet"</note>. Concerning this, see the **Fountain of Hadrian** <note>a reference to a specific alchemical authority or text, likely Hadrianus a Mynsicht or a similar source</note>.

115. The **third vessel** is called the **furnace**. This stands apart as it protects the other vessels along with the substance and the entire work. The masters also hide this among their secrets, keeping it for their own study.

116. The **Furnace of the Athanors** <note>original: "Furnus Altanorum"</note> is called the "Guardian" <note>original: "Custos"</note> or **athanor** because of the **immortal fire** <note>original: "ignis immortalis" — a fire kept burning at a constant temperature for long periods</note> which it constantly maintains. It provides a continuous fire, even if it is unequal at times; it provides heat to the work according to the quantity of the matter and the goal, whether the vessel requires a larger or smaller capacity to be managed.

117. The **material of the furnace** should be made of thick leather, or of rich earth, or the best **clay** <note>original: "argilla"</note>, or a shell made of mud mixed with **horse manure** <note>original: "fimo equino" — a common binding and insulating material in early laboratory furnaces</note> and hair. It should be prepared with oily mud to a uniform thickness of 3 or 4 finger-breadths, so that it might contain and reflect the heat as strongly as possible.

<meta>This page continues the technical specifications for alchemical apparatus, moving from the general concept of the furnace (athanor) to the specific construction details of its internal structure and monitoring features. It follows the description of the "blind gourd" and the "wooden incubator" from the previous page.</meta>

118. The shape of the **furnace** <note>original: "furni"</note> should be round, with an <unclear>internal</unclear> height of two feet, or thereabouts. In its middle, an **iron plate** <note>original: "lamina ferrea"</note> should be placed, which is also round and has the thickness of the back of a knife. It should occupy almost the entire width of the vessel itself, but it should be slightly narrower so that it does not touch the walls. It should be supported by three or four iron brackets fixed to the walls and should be pierced with numerous holes so that the **heat** <note>original: "calor"</note> may be more easily carried upward through them and through the space between the sides of the furnace and the round plate. 

Below the plate, a small door should be left, and another one above it in the same walls, so that the **fire** <note>original: "ignis"</note> may be placed underneath through the lower door, while the regulation of the heat may be perceived through the upper one. 

On the opposite side, a **small window** <note>original: "fenestella"</note> in a **diamond shape** <note>original: "figura rhomboidea"</note> should be made and fitted with glass, so that when an eye is pressed to it against the light, the colors may be observed. The aforementioned plate, with its <unclear>navel</unclear> <note>original: "umbilico" — likely referring to a central support or mounting point</note> and tripod <unclear>secretly</unclear>, should be placed with the double vessel. Finally, the furnace should be very well covered with its dome <note>original: "testudine" — literally "tortoise shell," referring to a vaulted or arched roof</note> or continuous, fitting roof, with both small doors strictly closed so that the heat does not escape.

119. The first path of the work has a goal: the **generation of two types of sulfur** <note>original: "duplicis sulfuris generatio" — in alchemy, "sulfur" often represents the active, masculine, or combustible principle of a substance</note>. The composition and perfection of both are fulfilled in this manner.

<meta>This page transitions from the physical description of the furnace on page 25 to the allegorical and chemical process occurring within the vessels. Take the **red dragon** <term>Draco ruffus; a symbol for raw mineral sulfur or antimony</term>, spirited and warlike, born of wicked sulfur. To this, let nothing be lacking from its native **Poland** <note>This likely refers to high-quality saltpeter or mineral ores sourced from Polish mines, which were famous in the 17th century</note>; then —
take seven or nine noble **eagles** <term>Aquilas; alchemical code for sublimated substances, often mercury or sal ammoniac</term>, whose eyesight is so sharp that it does not —
dim even before the rays of the sun. When they are properly in their **prisons** <note>original: "carceribus"; the sealed glass vessels or flasks</note>
closed and strongly shut with their companions, let a **bath** <note>original: "balneum"; specifically a water or vapor bath to provide gentle, indirect heat</note>
be placed beneath them. Under the influence of the warm vapor, they will strive to —
fight; they clash well and are worn down
in the contest, until at last, around the **fortieth** <gloss>40th</gloss>
day, the eagles begin to prey upon and tear themselves to pieces —
those monsters will infect the entire prison with their gore and **black poison** <note>This describes the "Nigredo" or blackening stage, where the matter undergoes putrefaction and turns into a dark slurry</note>, by which the bathed —
eagles are also forced to breathe. From the slaughter of this
**putrefaction** <term>putrefactionis; the essential stage of breaking down the material to its "first matter"</term>, the **raven** <term>corvus; the classic symbol for the black stage of the alchemical Great Work</term> will be generated. Little by little, 
as its head is blackened and the bath is somewhat 
closed off, it will soon stretch its wings and begin to fly 
until it is heavily weighed down by the winds and clouds.
It is driven about for a long time; then the **stars shine forth** <note>This refers to small, bright "scintillations" or crystals appearing on the surface of the blackened matter, signaling the end of the Nigredo</note>.
At last, by a long and gentle rain and **heavenly dew** <note>Symbolic of the condensed vapors falling back into the matter to wash it</note>
it is whitened; it will transition into the most brilliant white color.
But the raven is born from the hissing of two —
**dragons** heard during the whitening of the flesh. Distill the 
elements from the dragon according to the prescribed order 
until they are fixed in their own **earth** <term>terra; the solid residue or "body" at the bottom of the vessel</term> and end in a snowy and 
very fine powder. Once this is accomplished, 
you will have first attained your desire for the **white work** <note>The "White Work" or *albedo* is the stage where the alchemist produces the "White Stone," which was believed to be able to transmute base metals into silver</note>.

<meta>This page transitions from the previous discussion of the "Nigredo" (blackening) and the "Albedo" (whitening) into the final phase of the Great Work: the "Rubedo" or Reddening. While the previous page focused on the purification of the "eagles" (sublimates) in their "prisons" (vessels), this page details the transition from the lily-white stage to the blood-red perfection of the Philosopher's Stone. Although the source language was listed as French, the manuscript text provided is entirely in Latin.</meta>

120. If you intend to proceed further to the **Redness** <term>rubedo; the final stage of the alchemical Great Work representing perfection and solar heat</term>,
add the element of **Fire**, which is lacking in the **White Work** <note>original: "operi albo"; the Albedo stage which produces a white stone capable of transmuting metals into silver</note>, having moved the
vessel of Fire and strengthened the sensation of the **Fire** through its degrees
with a **Royal Hand** <note>original: "manu Regia"; a metaphor for the expert skill, control, and authority of the master alchemist</note>, until the hidden part
of the **Orpiment** <term>Auripigmenti; an arsenic sulfide mineral used here as a code for a specific chemical state</term> is made manifest, whose sweat shall be
the emerging lemon color for you. Let the **Fire of the fourth degree** <note>the highest and most intense level of heat, often compared to a direct flame or a blast furnace</note>
rule through its points, until the face of the work
attains a **purple rose** from a lily, and finally, 
having become like an **amaranth** <note>a mythical "everlasting" flower, representing the fixed and non-fading nature of the Red Stone</note> tinted with the redness of blood.
But do not cease to adorn the **Self-fire** <term>Autopyrum; likely referring to a self-sustaining heat or a specific type of furnace vessel</term> with the fire of Fire,
and you will see the reddest ashes, intangible to the touch,
be finished by the Hand. May this **Stone** <term>lapis; the Philosopher's Stone</term> in such a way 
raise your soul to greater things under the 
auspices of the **Holy Trinity**.

121. For those who, out of ignorance of the arts, believe 
they have brought the work to its goal through **sulfur alone**
and claim to have fulfilled the 
secret precepts, they are gravely 
deceived. But you, beware of the darkness in empty predictions,
for the **Stone** is completed by a **double work**.
The first is in creating the **sulfur**; the second is in 
making the **elixir**.

122. The **Sulfur of the Philosophers** is a most subtle earth,
a hot and dry mass, in whose belly our **Fire**
is abundantly multiplied and hidden; for this reason, 
it has earned the name **Fire of the Stone**, and it also possesses 
within itself the power of opening and penetrating the 
**bodies of metals** <note>original: "metallorum Corpora"</note> and changing them into its own temperament.

It emphasizes that the Stone is a "double work" involving both the creation of a philosophical sulfur and the final elixir capable of penetrating and transforming base metals.</summary>

...to be produced within their own limits, from which the nourishments <note>original: "foemetis," likely a corruption of "fomentis"; the materials that "feed" the alchemical fire</note> become clear; it is called **masculine** <note>In alchemy, the Red stage/Gold is considered masculine and solar, while the White stage/Silver is feminine and lunar</note>.

123. Lest we leave anything unattended, let the students of philosophy know that from the first offspring of **sulfur**, another can be born secondarily and multiplied infinitely. Nature—or rather the wise man—accurately guards the use of that **celestial fire** <note>original: "ignis illius coelestis"; often referring to the "secret fire" or the concentrated energy used to animate the matter</note> well. From whichever matter the sulfur <unclear>Ithom</unclear> is born, it is multiplied from the same by adding a small portion of the first. However, let the beginners seek all things in the works of Lambertus <note>likely referring to an alchemical author such as Lambertus de Monte</note> and the catalogs of **Llull** <note>Raymond Llull, a major figure in medieval alchemy</note>; let it suffice to have studied them.

124. The **Elixir** is composed of a **triple matter**: namely, the **water of metals** or **mercury** from the first **sublimate** <term>sublimato; a substance purified by heating it into a vapor that then solidifies</term>, the white or red **ferment** <term>fermento; a substance added to the work to "leaven" it and direct it toward silver (white) or gold (red)</term> according to how they assist the intention, and the second offspring of sulfur; everything is measured by weight.

125. In the perfect **Elixir**, there are five proper and our own qualities: that it be **fusible**, **permanent**, **penetrating**, **dyeing**, and **multiplying**. It takes its tincture <term>tincturam; the power to change the color and nature of a metal</term> and **fixation** <term>fixationem; the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat</term> from the ferment, its **penetration** from the sulfur, and its **fusion** from the **quicksilver** <term>argento vivo; another name for mercury</term>, which is the medium for joining the tincture (namely of the ferment and the sulfur). However, it borrows the virtue of **multiplication** from the spirit of the **quintessence** <term>quinta essentia; the "fifth element," a pure, incorruptible substance beyond the four physical elements</term>...

126. Two **perfect metals** <note>Gold and Silver</note> exhibit the perfect tincture because they are dyed by the pure nature of sulfur. Therefore, no ferment of metals should be sought outside these two bodies. It dyes, therefore, with the **Sun and Moon** <note>original: "solo et luna"; alchemical code for Gold and Silver</note> its white and red elixir.

Mercury must first receive various tinctures <note>original: "tincturis"; agents that imbue color and properties</note> in order to communicate another substance.

127 In composing the **elixir**, however, it must be known [how it] is taken or mixed, and each elixir rejoices under its own proper **ferment** <note>original: "fermento"; a substance, usually gold or silver, added to "leaven" the alchemical mass and direct its transformation</note> and desires its own specific attributes: for one is compared to these two luminaries <note>the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver)</note>, so that they may have their diverse substance and distinct tinctures.

129. **Tinctures** are the humors <note>original: "humores"; refers to the "vital fluids" or moisture within the matter, similar to the four humors of the human body</note> from the stone which must be extracted successively; they are driven and <unclear>restrained</unclear> and radically, therefore all the labor and care of the practitioner should be focused on these humors. Nor is the work improved from any other source if the operation of the stone is circulated without the moisture; for it is necessary for the whole mass and the humors to first be dissolved and liquefied along the way. However, since the **radical moisture** <term>humor radicalis; the fundamental, life-sustaining moisture believed to exist in all matter</term> has the quality of fire, it is most tenacious; because it is bound to the <unclear>muddy</unclear> watery part, it is not easily separated. Therefore, extract those humors gradually and successively through its own **wheel** <note>original: "per rotam suam"; the "Wheel of the Philosophers" refers to the repeated cycle of distillation, sublimation, or dissolution and coagulation</note> by **dissolving** and **congealing**; for by the multiple repetition of alternating dissolution and congelation, the wheel <unclear>revolves</unclear> and the entire work is finished.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Red Work (Rubedo), specifically detailing the final fixation of the substance into a stable "Elixir" and the method for testing its perfection on a hot plate.</meta>

130. <insert>+</insert> Finally, the **elixirs** <note>original: "elixiria"; the final medicinal or transmutative substances sought by the alchemist</note> consist of a union of the dry, fixed element and the moist element, forming an indissoluble marriage <note>A common alchemical metaphor for the chemical bonding of two opposite principles, such as Sulfur and Mercury</note>, so that they may never be separated. Instead, through a constant, moderate heat, let the fixed remain within the moist until they endure the fire together. A sign of perfection is if a tiny bit of it, when cast upon a glowing hot iron or bronze plate, flows immediately without smoke.

131. Take one part of **red earth** <term>terra rubra; the solid, stabilized matter achieved at the final stage of the Great Work</term>, or the fixed substance, three weights of the lamb <note>original: "agni"; this may be a symbolic reference to the "Lamb of God" or "Aries," or a corruption of a specific weight unit</note> and add a double weight. Let them be ground and mixed together very well so that an **amalgam** <note>A soft, blendable mixture, usually of a metal and mercury, resembling a paste</note> is made in the likeness of butter or a metallic paste. Immediately, with the earth wrapped in fixed **tartar** <note>original: "tartari fixi"; potassium carbonate derived from wine lees, often used as a flux to help solids melt</note> <unclear>antiphilibis</unclear>, add one weight, half of which is placed in its own vessel, most tightly sealed; let them be **digested** <note>The process of slow, gentle heating, similar to incubation, to mature the substance</note> with fire of the first degree until the matter is dried. Then, let them be raised through the proper **degrees of fire** <term>ignis gradus; the traditional four stages of increasing heat used to transition the matter through various chemical states</term>. It is a marvelous operation, where with "joyful fear" those things that were lacking are fixed in their own earth. Immediately, no more **volatiles** <note>original: "volatilia"; substances that easily vaporize or "fly away" when heated</note> can be seen; the matter will finally terminate in a bright red and transparent state from above. Its parts are added to the whole earth and projected into the "worm" <note>original: "lumbricum"; likely referring to the coiled cooling tube of a still, or a specific visual shape the melting matter takes</note> of water, drop by drop with its oil, until it is entirely melted and flows without smoke. It no longer fears flight <note>evaporation or loss of substance through heat</note>, but rather, wrapped in all the sweetness of the earth, it causes the inner parts it has received to remain stable with its fixed components.

...to receive first the **mercury** <note>original: "mercurium"; here referring to the "mercury of the philosophers," the fluid, transformative principle of the work</note> of the entire **tincture** <term>tincturae; the concentrated essence capable of transforming or coloring base metals into gold or silver</term>, it communicates another substance.

127 In composing **oils** <note>original: "oleis"; in alchemy, "oils" usually refer to the essential, flammable, or sulfurous extracts of a substance</note>, one must be careful that they are not [incorrectly] mixed or mingled, for each of the oils rejoices under its own **ferment** <term>fermento; a substance that initiates a chemical or spiritual transformation in the matter, similar to how leaven works on dough</term> and requires its own elements: for both are compared to the **two luminaries** <note>original: "duo luminaria"; the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver)</note>, having their own diverse substances and distinct tinctures.

129. The great mastery [is derived] from the **noble stone** <note>original: "lapide generoso"; the primary material or the Philosophers' Stone in an intermediate state</note> which must be extracted successively; they act through both whiteness and the **radical** [moisture]. Therefore, let all the labor and care of the worker be directed toward the moisture. Nor should he notice otherwise if the work is circulated from the stone besides the moisture, for it is necessary that the moisture be first resolved and liquefied. Furthermore, the **radical moisture** <term>humor radicalis; the essential, life-giving fluid or "innate heat" believed to reside within all matter</term> is extremely tenacious against the fire, because it is bound to the **mud** <unclear>luto</unclear> <note>original: "luto"; likely referring to the base, earthy dregs or the "body" of the matter</note> from which it is not easily separated. Therefore, act so that you extract that moisture gradually and successively throughout its entirety by **dissolving and congealing** <note>original: "solvendo et congelando"; the central alchemical dictum of *Solve et Coagula*, referring to the breaking down and reforming of matter</note>; for by many alternating turns of dissolution and congelation, the whole will be extracted and the entire work is finished.

130. The perfection of the <term>elixir</term> <note>the ultimate transformative substance</note> consists in a dry thirst and the union of the moist, and in an indissoluble marriage, so that they are never separated. Instead, they should flow into a liquid state through a very moderate heat and endure and remain through every fire. Indeed, that perfection is a thirst of that [matter] glowing above the <term>luminary</term> <note>luminare; likely referring to the Sun or gold</note>; with that same [luminary], let it flow instantly as a glowing cloak without smoke.

131. The operation is marvelous: how, by retreating out of fear, the <term>volatiles</term> <note>volatilia; substances that easily evaporate or escape when heated</note> are constrained and fixed in their own earth, so that in no way can they be stirred up. The matter will finally be limited to an illuminated, red, and transparent upper [state], whose parts will receive the whole and go forth into a bright fire. Gently, drop by drop, let it be drunk and stirred with its own red oil until the whole is melted and flows without smoke. Nor should it fear flight <note>evaporation or loss of substance</note>; for indeed, being wrapped in the sweetness of the earth, it stands and acts to receive the inner parts of its companion.

The king’s cycle is completed through unity; hold it and guard it, and by guarding it in God, you shall then rejoice. <note>original: "Anni regis completur per unum, et habeto et custodito, custodi in deo gaudeas tunc." 132. 133. The corrupt "accidents" <note>external, non-essential qualities or impurities</note> of the bodies, which besieged their purity and true light with the shadows of nature, have been so removed through long and repeated <term>sublimations</term> <note>purifying a solid by turning it into vapor and back into solid</note> and <term>digestions</term> <note>prolonged gentle heating</note> that the fire of nature—freed from its chains and strengthened by the help of heavenly powers—acts most powerfully when enclosed in this, our <term>fifth element</term>. Therefore, it should be no surprise if the elixir obtains the power not only to perfect the imperfect, but also the power to multiply its own strength. Now, the source of this <term>multiplication</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity and potency of the alchemical medicine</note> is in the <term>Prince of Luminaries</term> <note>the Sun, often associated with the source of all growth and the "gold" of the heavens</note>, who, by the infinite multiplication of his rays, [influences] everything in this world of ours...

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the "multiplication" of the Elixir—the process of increasing its potency and quantity. Following the discussion of the Quintessence and the stabilization of the White and Red Elixirs, the text now details specific laboratory procedures for the "Red Work" (the goal of creating gold).</meta>

...the mass in graduated stages multiplies, it increases its multiplicative power <note>original: "virtutem multiplicatincam," likely a scribal error for "multiplicativam," meaning the ability of the substance to transform other matter</note> by preparing with similar things.

134. There are three ways to multiply the Elixir. In the first way: Take one weight of the <term>Red Elixir</term> <note>the final goal of the alchemist, capable of transmuting base metals into gold</note>, mix it with nine weights of its own "red water," and dissolve it into a liquid in a dissolving vessel. Once it is perfectly dissolved and united, coagulate it by simmering over a slow fire until it hardens into a "bath" or a red plate. Afterward, moisten it with its own "red oil" in the prescribed manner until it flows. In this way, you will possess a medicine ten times more powerful than the first; the task is completed easily and in a short time.

135. In the second way: Take a portion of the elixir at will, combined with its water. Maintaining the correct weights, seal it perfectly in a vessel of solution. Dissolve it through <term>inhumation</term> <note>the practice of burying a sealed vessel in warm horse dung or sand to provide a gentle, constant heat</note> in a bath. Distill the dissolved matter, and by processing the remains successively through the proper degrees of fire—fixing them as was done in the first and second stages of the work—continue until it turns to stone. Finally, stir and project <note>the act of "projection" involves casting the finished elixir onto molten base metal to transform it</note> it. This path is longer but richer, for the power of the Elixir is increased a hundredfold. For the more subtle it becomes through repeated operation, the more it retains the powers of the <term>superior and inferior</term> <note>a reference to the Emerald Tablet's "as above, so below" principle, suggesting the Elixir bridges the celestial and terrestrial worlds</note> and works more powerfully.

<summary>This page outlines two specific methods for "multiplying" the Red Elixir, increasing its transmutive power ten-fold or even a hundred-fold through repeated dissolution and coagulation. The instructions emphasize the use of "red water" and "red oil" alongside controlled heating techniques like inhumation.</summary>
