Take one third part of the **medicine** <term>medicina: In alchemy, this refers to the Philosopher’s Stone or the transformative agent in its final stages of preparation.</term> and place it in an earthen crucible over a light fire. Immediately when it feels the heat, add its **white oil** drop by drop, one after another. It will enter the substance thoroughly, acting as a true medicine. 

Test its fusibility upon a glowing copper plate: if it dissolves into a white substance without smoke, the medicine is created. If not, remove it from the heat to cool, dripping its oil upon it drop by drop until it shows the sign mentioned above in the prescribed manner. 

Afterward, allow it to cool, for the elixir is complete—a price beyond price—which converts that which is diminished and imperfect in the [metallic] body into infinite, light, and good **silver-making** <note>original: "lunificam," meaning "moon-making." In alchemy, the Moon always represents Silver.</note> substance. 

Therefore, **project** <term>proiice: The act of 'projection,' where a tiny amount of the finished Elixir is cast onto a mass of molten base metal to transform it.</term> one weight of this upon 1,000 parts of **quicksilver** <term>argenti vivi: Literally "living silver," the Latin term for mercury.</term> that has been washed in the common way with salt and vinegar. It will be converted into pure **silver** <note>original: "lunam" (the Moon).</note>, better than that from a mine, as proven by every test. And know that if it is projected upon any of the imperfect bodies, it will be transformed into quicksilver immediately after reaching its **radical moisture** <term>humiditatem radicalem: The fundamental, "root" moisture of a substance, thought to be the essential fluid that preserves its form and life.</term>.

# On the Imbibition of the Nutritive Moisture, etc.
## Concerning the Nutritive
### Chapter 27

**Imbibition** <term>Imbibatio: From the Latin 'imbibere' (to drink in). In alchemy, this is the process where a dry "body" or powder is slowly saturated with a liquid (moisture) to soften or nourish it without drowning it.</term> of the nutritive moisture and the nourishment of the ground is nothing other than adapting the nutritional temperament proportionally to the qualities of the **Nourished substance**. It must not be too cold or too hot, nor too bright or too **dark** <note>original: "lortum," likely a variant of "luridum" (dark/pale) or "luteum" (muddy), used here to contrast with "lucidum" (bright/clear).</note>. For if it is too cold, it seems to kill the **virtue** <note>In this context, "virtue" refers to the inherent life-force or transformative power of the alchemical substance.</note>; if it is too hot, it suffocates and extinguishes the **digestive virtue** <term>digestiva virtus: The internal heat or energy that matures the substance, much like digestion in the human stomach.</term>. Therefore, the work—which arises from the generative power of **sulfur** and **quicksilver** <term>argenti vivi: Literally "living silver," the standard term for mercury.</term>—must have the power of heating and moistening according to reason and capability.

Nutritional things act either by their own nature or by accident. Those that heat and moisten by their own nature are two things: one is like an efficient principle, namely a perfect **digestion** <term>digestio: The alchemical process of heating a substance at a steady, low temperature to bring it to maturity.</term> such as **Nature** performs. And likewise, in one hour, it produces one [result] through the backward movement of much darkness into sufficient brightness, and through successive progression, it turns much brightness into sufficient darkness. The other is like a medicinal book—one that is skillful and fit to generate **choler** <term>choleram: One of the four humors (yellow bile), associated with the qualities of being hot and dry.</term> by heating and drying, and consequently by binding the substances together.

<summary>This chapter explains the theory of "imbibition," the process of nourishing the alchemical elixir with balanced heat and moisture. It emphasizes that the "digestive virtue" of the stone must be carefully maintained to avoid killing its transformative power.</summary>

<keywords>imbibition, nutritive moisture, sulfur, quicksilver, digestive virtue, alchemy, temperament, choler</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the alchemical "nutrition" of the elixir from Chapter 27 on page 62. It specifically explains how the "moisture" or "phlegm" acts as a transformative agent, likened to milk turning into blood, to strengthen the "medicinal body" of the stone.</meta>

Phlegm is that moisture which, unless it is held back from turning into smoke <note>original: "fumi"</note> through cooling, is called the **water against nature** <note>original: "aqua contra naturam". In alchemy, this is a specialized solvent or substance that possesses properties opposite to ordinary water, often used to dissolve or "kill" the metallic bodies.</note>. In this way, it is fit and able to generate a nutritional and nutritive moisture, which is like **milk** that is converted into **blood**. For then, according to the exhalation <term>exubilationem: A term referring to the process of vapors rising, refinement, or a kind of "boiling over" of the spirit from the matter.</term> of the exhaled moisture, its heat—by its known change—heats and moistens the **quicksilver** <term>argentum vivum: literally "living silver," the standard term for mercury.</term> with moisture.

And for this reason, it is called "unnatural" <note>original: "innaturalis"</note>; in such a way, it heats and moistens the quicksilver, which is the **food of sulfur**. This process is so significant that it constitutes the generation of **spiritual blood** <note>This refers to the vital, transformative liquid that nourishes the alchemical "body" or stone as it matures.</note>. Because from the substance of the blood, whatever moisture is exhaled, a spirit is generated within the substance of the **medicinal body** <note>The "body" of the philosopher's stone during its preparation.</note>, and by that spirit, the aforementioned substance is nutritionally strengthened.

Therefore, my son, you should understand within yourself these things: that for the **multiplication** <term>multiplicationem: The alchemical stage where the power and quantity of the philosopher's stone are increased.</term> of sulfur and quicksilver, their perfect exhalation and digestion are necessary, along with a greater and more abundant suitability for the hope of generating blood. However, those forms of **quicksilver** which by their own nature are not fit or able to generate the absolute life-giving hope—as is the case with all types of metals on their own—act as accidental multipliers, generating and providing for generation in others, just as water nourishes vegetables by its nature.

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process of "nutrition," where a specialized moisture (phlegm or "water against nature") acts like milk turning into blood to feed the sulfur and quicksilver. This "spiritual blood" is essential for the maturation and multiplication of the medicinal body of the philosopher's stone.</summary>

### On the Composition of the Red Elixir
## Chapter 28

In such a way we are accustomed to practice thoroughly: just as the elixir for the "white" <note>The "White Work" or *albedo* is the penultimate stage of alchemy, producing a stone that turns metals into silver.</note> was made, so make the elixir for the "red" <note>The "Red Work" or *rubedo* is the final stage, producing the Philosopher's Stone that turns metals into gold.</note>. There is no difference in the practice except for this: let the red end "consume," and just as the white is multiplied, the **sulfur** <note>Sulfur represents the active, fiery, "masculine" principle in the work.</note> shall multiply the red, by placing fine sulfur in the place of **quicksilver** <term>argenti-vivi: mercury</term>. 

And let the water of mercury first be sublimated with the fire of the stone through boiling alone; and let the **medicine** <note>Another name for the Elixir or Stone.</note> be composed of three parts of **ferment** <note>The agent that triggers the "leavening" or transformation of the base matter.</note>, three of water, three of **air**, and one and a half parts of **fire** <note>These "elements" refer to specific alchemical states or fractions of the substance rather than literal air or flame.</note>. And [continue] for the reduction of the air and its sublimation, until the whole is fixed <note>original: "fixetur." Fixation is the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat.</note> together. 

Afterward, continue the three fires which we spoke of for the white work, so that the elixir may be better fixed and purified through the best **digestion** <term>digestionem: a process of slow, continuous heating in a sealed vessel, similar to incubation</term>. My son, this excellent digestion gives the medicine the power and the action it must perform, because through it, moisture is brought to an end and the fire digests; then the elixir shall be drawn out, just as described above, through the course of a light fire without fear.

<vocab>elixir, sulfur, mercurius, fermentatio, digestio, alchimia</vocab>

<keywords>Red Elixir, Rubedo, Sulfur, Quicksilver, Fixation, Alchemical Digestion, Temperament, Fermentation</keywords>

### How the medicine multiplies itself for the good and for the better, and concerning its nature
### Chapter 30

When it is said in the writings concerning the **medicine** <note>original: "medicina." Another name for the Philosopher's Stone or the Elixir.</note> and the power of **projection**, know that its virtue can be artificially multiplied in two ways. 

The first way is that you cause it to be dissolved in the **water of white mercury** <note>original: "aqua mercurii albi." A specialized solvent, likely a purified mercurial solution used to break down the medicine for refinement.</note>—even the red medicine itself—from which you proceed until it is made into clear water. Afterward, in a gentle **decoction** <note>original: "decoctione." A process of slow, steady heating in a liquid medium.</note>, cause it to coagulate, and finally, let the oil be incinerated over the fire we have often mentioned, until it flows well upon it.

# On Multiplication

We have spoken of the plates, and why we addressed them specifically. The power will be doubled in the **tincture** <note>original: "tinctura." In alchemy, this is the concentrated essence of the Stone that "colors" or transforms base metals into gold or silver.</note> and in all perfections, as you will be able to see in these **projections** <term>projection: The final act of the alchemical work, where a small amount of the finished "Medicine" or "Stone" is cast upon molten base metal to transform it.</term>; for in the total, the weight that was previously projected upon 1,000 will now be projected upon 10,000. And if this name of **multiplication** <term>multiplication: The process of increasing the power and quantity of the alchemical medicine through repeated cycles of dissolution and refinement.</term> is a great **labor** <note>original: "poena." While this literally means punishment or pain, in alchemical texts it refers to the arduous "suffering" or transformation the matter must undergo.</note>, the second labor of the greater multiplication is even more significant. 

This is because if it is distilled in such a way after it has been dissolved into water, the power of its **mercury** <term>mercury: Not the common metal, but the "spirit" or volatile principle extracted from the alchemical substance.</term> will multiply it a hundredfold; that is to say, one weight will be worth 100. Now, the method of multiplication is that you must dissolve each individual species separately in its own "water" by means of **inhumation** <note>original: "inhumationem." The practice of burying the glass vessel in warm earth or horse dung to provide a gentle, constant heat resembling a womb.</note>. 

Afterward, you shall separate the remains through **distillation** <note>original: "distillationem." The process of purifying a liquid by boiling it and condensing its vapors.</note>, receiving first the water, and then the other substance as has been described. There will remain at the bottom a **fixed earth** <note>original: "terra fixa." The stable, solid part of the matter that has been purified and can now withstand the highest heat of the furnace.</note>, a clear substance in the form of a powder. You shall then pour the water back over it after distillation until the earth has "drunk" it all, and until all that water is made **fixed** <note>original: "fixa." To make a volatile substance stable so it no longer evaporates when heated.</note> with that earth, and balanced with its **oil** <note>original: "oleo." The "soul" or oily essence of the material.</note>, and in its tincture, until the whole is fixed together and the whole melts upon the plate, etc.

From this **medicine** <note>original: "medicina." Another name for the Philosopher's Stone, reflecting its power to "heal" imperfect metals into gold.</note>, the projection of one weight upon any **volatile bodies** <note>original: "corpora volubilia." Usually refers to base metals like lead or mercury that "flee" the fire by melting or evaporating.</note> converts them; and it certainly provides a tincture <unclear>with</unclear> <unclear>radiance</unclear> multiplied by 100 virtues in its powers in such a way that if one weight first converted 1,000, it will now convert 10,000. If it is prepared a third time as has been said, it will then convert 100,000, and so proceeding unto infinity. 

Therefore, my son, you must note that the more the medicine is **refined** <note>original: "subtiliata." Rendered "subtle" or spiritualized through chemical processing.</note>—that is, dissolved, sublimated, and congealed—the more...

<keywords>multiplication, tincture, projection, medicine, distillation, fixed earth, volatile bodies, refinement, alchemy</keywords>

Therefore, son, do not be weary of repeating the dissolution, **sublimation** <term>sublimation: Heating a substance until it turns to vapor, then collecting it as a solid again to achieve high purity.</term>, and **coagulation** <term>coagulation: The process of solidifying a liquid, often through cooling or evaporation.</term>; for through such operations the **medicine** <note>original: "medicina." In alchemy, this is the finished substance, the Philosopher's Stone in its potent form.</note> is digested, united, multiplied in power, perfected, activated, and fixed <note>original: "fixatur." Fixation is the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat so that it no longer evaporates.</note>. Wherefore, son, if you dissolve and congeal well as we have said, and if you recognize how the spirit ought to operate, you will arrive at the perfection of our **Magisterium** <term>Magisterium: The 'Great Work' or the total mastery of the alchemical art.</term>. 

Be well-informed that no truth can be explained as we have told it to you; but if you are a good investigator and a son of subtle learning, through what we have said and will say in our book of this present **Testament** <note>original: "testamenti." This likely refers to the *Testamentum*, a famous work attributed to the legendary alchemist Ramon Llull.</note> and **Codicil** <note>original: "codicilli." Therefore, let there be a holy silence, hidden by God’s gift for future times.

### Chapter 31: On the Multiplication of the Medicine in Quantity

And to perform the projection upon our other medicine, take one ounce of the said second medicine.

...which things it is very necessary to know from one’s conscience, regarding what some of the **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophorum." In alchemical literature, "Philosophers" refers to the ancient and medieval authorities of the art, such as Hermes Trismegistus, Geber, or Arnald of Villanova.</note> have spoken concerning the aforementioned **medicine** <term>medicine: The alchemical Elixir or Philosopher's Stone, believed to "cure" base metals into gold and restore human health.</term>.

# Concerning the Recapitulation of the Mastery in the Form of a Division
### Chapter 32.

Son, this **mastery** <term>mastery: (Latin: magisterium) The systematic procedure or "Great Work" required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</term> has steps and is divided; the first is divided and has the principal **dissolutions** <note>original: "dissolutiones." The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state to begin its purification.</note>. Through the first step, you must purify the **stone** <term>stone: The Philosopher's Stone in its developing stages.</term> and make the **fixed volatile** <note>This refers to the process of "opening" a solid substance so that it may be transformed or purified through evaporation.</note>. 

The second part is divided and has two principal reductions, and through this middle step, the stone is prepared and becomes **fixed** <term>fixed: (Latin: fixatur) Rendering a substance stable so that it no longer evaporates or changes when exposed to high heat.</term> until it can rest and endure the pressure of the fire. 

The third step is formed from the two steps mentioned above, and therefore it is divided into two parts. The first is **solution and congealing** <term>solution and congealing: (Latin: solutio et congelatio) The repetitive cycle of liquefying and solidifying the substance to increase its purity and power.</term>; the second is **fixation**. And through this third step, the stone is completed, and its **multiplication** <term>multiplication: (Latin: multiplicatio) The final stage of the work where the potency or quantity of the Stone is increased so it can transmute larger amounts of metal.</term> is performed by repeating the operation of the first step.

->the end<-

<summary>This page outlines a three-stage summary of the alchemical process, focusing on the transition of the "Stone" from a volatile to a fixed state. It concludes the chapter by explaining how the finished Stone can be further empowered through the process of multiplication.</summary>
<keywords>Mastery, Magisterium, Philosopher's Stone, dissolution, fixation, multiplication, alchemical steps, purification, volatile, solution and congealing</keywords>

#### of the Ancient Testament written above <note>original: "Antiquii Testamenti suprascripti." This likely refers to a preceding section of the manuscript or a specific foundational text in the Lullian corpus.</note>

**Corruption** <term>corruption: (Latin: corruptio) The breaking down or decomposition of a substance’s current form so it can be reborn in a purified state.</term> and **Purification** <term>purification: (Latin: depuratio) The cleansing of a substance, removing "superfluities" or impurities.</term> occur at the same time by withdrawing a part of the Corrupted Cause (namely the essence). ### Concerning corruption through the second wheel

Then, perform the second circular wheel <note>The "wheel" (Latin: rota) is a common alchemical metaphor for a repeating cycle of chemical operations, such as distillation and condensation, or the "rotation" of the four elements.</note> upon the sterile body that has passed through the fine filter, in a **digestion** <term>digestion: (Latin: digestio) A process of subjecting a substance to gentle, continuous heat, similar to the warmth of a stomach, to mature or ripen it.</term> similar to the first, which is the digestion of perfection. 1. **Grinding** <term>grinding: (Latin: contritio) The mechanical breaking down of materials into a fine powder.</term>
2. **Imbibition** <term>imbibition: (Latin: ambibitio/imbibitio) The process where a solid substance absorbs a liquid until it is saturated.</term>
3. **Decoction**
4. **Solution** 

These are performed by the **fire of nature** <note>original: "ignem Nae" (ignem naturae); a gentle, natural heat, often compared to the warmth of a brooding hen or the sun, as opposed to a violent, destructive flame.</note> and not by way of water, except when separating the coarse from the subtle and the earth from the compound for the purpose of testing the whole. For when [the earth] is full, it blocks the following path and hinders the achievement of the perfect act. Therefore, we refine it through **levigation** <term>levigation: (Latin: levigatione) The process of grinding a substance into a very fine, smooth powder, often while suspended in a liquid.</term>, and we separate the light from the heavy and vice versa, without overstepping the bounds of its own proper range, but according to what nature and its power require.

### The Putrefaction of the Compound

This is established so that the perfected **essence** <term>essence: (Latin: essentia) The inner nature or purest part of a substance, separated from its physical impurities.</term> may be better separated from the putrid parts and raised upward to the heights through **sublimation** <term>sublimation: (Latin: sublimatio) The process where a substance is heated into a vapor and then collects as a solid or liquid higher up in the vessel, leaving impurities behind.</term>, which is afterwards carried into the completed sediment.

## Separation of Itself from This

When you see that no more water can be obtained by the heat of the bath <term>bath: (Latin: *balneum*) A water bath or "bain-marie," used to provide gentle, even heat.</term>, let this be a sign to you that you possess the art and must separate the substance from the fire. This is because the [remaining] vapor is hotter and of greater perfection, since it holds something of the **fixed** <term>fixed: (Latin: *fixum*) In alchemy, a substance that is stable under heat and does not evaporate.</term> part bound to it through the uniformity of the dissolution. Wherefore the fire is required to correspond to its proper point; separate it, therefore, from the fire by means of an appropriate fire, built up by degrees just as we have explained to you elsewhere. 

This is the **ferment** <term>ferment: (Latin: *fermentum*) An agent that "leavens" or triggers a transformation in the alchemical matter, similar to how yeast works in bread.</term> named above, made from two natures, from which is composed a "middle virtue" <note>This "middle virtue" refers to a balanced state that mediates between the heavy, solid "fixed" elements and the light, gaseous "volatile" elements.</note> which lies between the extremes of the **fixed** and the **volatile** <term>volatile: (Latin: *volatilia*) Substances that easily vaporize or evaporate when heated.</term>, from which all salt shall be produced artificially.

Then, through the helm <note>original: *limbum*, referring to the "limbeck" or the head of the distilling apparatus.</note>, distill the Fire by continuing uniformly until nothing...

### Strengthening of the Elements

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical process of "reduction" and "rectification," following the separation and sevenfold distillation of the elements described on the previous page. I do not wish to commit everything to writing, but rather to provide a long summary of the work that must be noted concerning its placement; for in the round circle, all is contained. This is understood through the established principles of the Art <note>original: "scituta artis" — The "Art" is a common term for alchemy, referring to the skilled manipulation of nature.</note>.

The beginning of the **reduction** <term>reduction: In alchemy, "reductio" refers to "leading back" a substance to its primal or restorative state, often by returning a distilled spirit to its solid base.</term> must be performed through the application of a higher **digestion** <term>digestion: The process of applying gentle, consistent heat over time to mature or "ripen" a substance, mimicking the heat of the stomach or the earth.</term> according to the **theory** <note>original: "theoricam" — Alchemical texts often distinguished between the practical work (practica) and the philosophical understanding (theorica).</note>.

When the materials that have been returned <note>original: "recurrita" — referring to the substances that have been cycled through the apparatus</note> have been rectified, join a portion of the **fixed** <note>The "fixed" part of a substance is that which resists the heat of the fire and does not evaporate.</note> (namely, from the "earth of fire") to the common matter through **calcination** <term>calcination: Heating a substance to high temperatures until it is reduced to a fine powder or ash.</term> with the **volatile moisture** <note>original: "humido volatili" — the liquid or gaseous part of the substance that evaporates easily.</note> in order to dissolve it. For this reason, we speak to you of the density of the matter, and you should understand the doctrine we gave you through the theory: namely, do not bypass the great perfection of uniformity that comes from **quicksilver** <note>original: "argento vivo" — Mercury, considered by alchemists to be the principle of fluidity and metallic perfection.</note>.

And understand the principle of your **reduction**, which you perform through the arrangement of your noble substance. This is understood because it has been very well **sublimated** <term>sublimation: A process where a solid is heated until it turns into vapor and then collects again as a solid in the cooler part of the vessel, used to purify the substance.</term> from the aforementioned earth, which is the "first cause" of your compound. From this, some parts are **unifying** <note>original: "coniunctivae"</note> and others are **dividing** <note>original: "divisivae"</note>. Those which unify belong to the true **essence** <note>original: "essentia"</note> of the base compound; but because the others are not of that origin, we say to join the naturally pure parts. This alone achieves the work, for it draws through an attractive power.

<summary>This page explains the "reduction" phase of the alchemical work, focusing on the combination of "fixed" earth and "volatile" moisture. It emphasizes the importance of mercury (quicksilver) in achieving a uniform, purified substance through sublimation and digestion.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical process of "reduction" and "rectification," specifically focusing on the internal separation of essence from foreign matter and the transition into the "Practice of Congealing."</meta>

That which belongs to its own **essence** <term>essence: In alchemy, the "essentia" is the fundamental, pure nature of a substance, often described as its "soul" or its most concentrated, active form.</term> is released by returning through a **hidden power** <note>original: "virtutem occultam" — In this context, "occult" simply means hidden from the naked eye, referring to the natural internal forces within matter.</note> into all that which does not belong to its own composite. This is because separation can never be achieved manually <note>original: "manualiter" — by hand or through simple physical sorting.</note>, though many people believe they know how. However, when the proper and appropriate method is understood through great intellect—because the parts and the durations in which they are **tortured by fire** <note>Alchemists used dramatic terms like "torture" (cruciantur) or "vexation" (vexantur) to describe the process of applying intense heat to break down and refine a substance.</note> are so thoroughly refined—no separation can occur except by that force which draws into its own center that which is of its own essence.

The foreign matter then remains fixed, for it is certain that nature wills it so in order to reach its perfection. When it conceives the highest fire in its center, it joins with the **volatiles** <note>The "volatiles" are the components of the substance that easily evaporate or turn into gas when heated.</note>. Afterward, the whole is separated from the **dark cause** (the vile residue) <note>original: "causa obscura (limbo vili)" — This likely refers to the "caput mortuum" or the "dead head," the useless dregs left at the bottom of the vessel after distillation.</note> which is understood to be the remains, and it must settle through **sublimation** <term>sublimation: The process where a solid substance is heated and turns directly into vapor, which then cools and solidifies again on a higher surface, leaving impurities behind.</term>.

### Practice of congealing through the way
### of reduction

If you understand the aforementioned causes, you can easily **congeal** <term>congealing: In alchemy, "congelatio" is the process of turning a liquid or spirit back into a solid or stable form, often by "fixing" it to a base.</term> at your will. For just as a substance is dissolved, so it is congealed by observing the opposite action. Therefore, take some of the **separated water** <note>original: "aqua separata" — Referring to the purified liquid or "spirit" distilled in the earlier phases of the work.</note> and soak the "earth" with it, acting according to the proportion of its power (namely, not just a little).

## On the Construction of the Sublimation Furnace

Perhaps you should gently draw out the base <note>original: "fundum" — the bottom or support structure of the apparatus.</note> which may withstand a strong fire, and you will place that base above the furnace, either by building up the walls of the furnace or in any other way so that it enters the furnace through the broken surface; and thus it is well sealed <note>original: "lertatur" — likely a scribal error for "lutatur," meaning to seal with *lute*, a paste made of clay, dung, and hair used to make joints airtight.</note> against the base and well-fitted to the furnace. Afterwards, place the aforementioned base made of earth—because of the great harshness of the glass <note>original: "vitio" — likely a transcription error for "vitrio" (glass); the text describes the danger of the glass failing under heat.</note> whose base is thus opened—which is thus supported without a [glass] base; in its opening is the width and breadth of the earthen base. 

Therefore, it is right to build up uniformly on the said earthen base while it is placed on top and well-joined. These vessels, sealed with clay and joined with the casing <note>original: "cassa" — a protective outer box or frame used to shield the glass from direct contact with the coals.</note> placed over the opening of the glass vessel, should have a height of two cubits <note>original: "alicubito" — a measurement of length, roughly the distance from the elbow to the fingertips.</note>. 

Afterwards, a bed of coals should be made in a circle around the clay sealing of the vessel from above, until the glass vessel is tempered by being heated. (I believe this is done to preserve the clay sealing and to first heat the glass so that it does not break when the flame—which must be produced in the furnace—strikes the vessel; if it were cold where the "flower of the fire" <note>original: "flos ignis" — a poetic term for the hottest part of the flame.</note> reaches the seam, it would shatter). 

Afterward, make the fire in the furnace with a flame. In this, be cautious, because there is a need for as strong and great a fire in the opening as you can manage. The substance will not be sublimated <note>term: <gloss>sublimation</gloss> — the process where a solid is heated until it turns directly into a vapor, then cools and solidifies again at the top of the vessel, leaving impurities behind.</note> except by the strongest fire, because it exists between the **fixed** <note>In alchemy, "fixed" substances resist fire and do not evaporate.</note> and the **volatile** <note>Substances that easily turn into gas or spirit when heated.</note>. But with the strongest fire, it will be sublimated; for this reason, the part that is more fixed is always better preserved in the casing, having been separated from its "sisters" <note>original: "sororibus" — a common alchemical metaphor for the impurities or related elements that are separated during purification.</note>.

<summary>This page provides detailed technical instructions on building a furnace for sublimation, emphasizing the use of an earthen base and protective "luting" to prevent glass vessels from shattering under extreme heat. It explains the necessity of a very high-intensity fire to drive the transition of a substance from a fixed to a volatile state.</summary>

<keywords>sublimation, furnace construction, luting, fixed and volatile, glass tempering, alchemical vessels, heat management</keywords>

And the more volatile substance always rises higher according to the point of the graduated fire <note>original: "ignis graduati" — a fire applied in controlled, increasing stages of intensity</note> in correspondence with its own nature. Therefore, I say that what is finally sublimated through a slow degree <note>a low or gradual heat</note> overcomes gold <note>original symbol: $\odot$</note> and is congealed. This occurs because this is the true substance of mercury, sublimated while preserving its own moisture, having been converted into a pure, non-burning sulfur <note>original: "sulfur non urens" — a philosophical sulfur that provides the "seed" of the metal without being consumed by fire</note> made by the Creator from an indeterminable substance.

This is the "middle substance"; this is the "weighty virtue"; this is the "measured form" which we described to you above in the chapter concerning the noble matter, which is the cause of Great Perfection. [The dregs] remain in the bottom of the vessel, possessing the nature of lead <note>original symbol: $\hbar$</note> and silver <note>original symbol: $\leftmoon$</note>, separated from the gold <note>original symbol: $\odot$</note>; these are contrary to the nature [of the work]. Therefore, cast them away as useless, for they are nothing but trouble and confusion.

There are some who, in order to better simplify and purify <note>original: "emiendum" — likely a scribal error for "emundandum," meaning to cleanse or refine</note> the aforementioned substance, elevate and sublimate it a second time. Wherefore, if you wish to sublimate, do so without dregs <note>original: "fætibus," likely "faecibus" — the waste or "feces" of the chemical reaction</note> using a fire appropriate to the requirements of its specific property.

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion of the chemical marriage of substances. ### At all times of Congelation, something is fixed §

Therefore it is much closer to **fixation** <term>fixatio|The process of making a volatile substance stable in heat so that it no longer evaporates or "flees" the fire.</term>—because in the said **matter** <note>original: "materia"</note>, the **volatile** cause is bound, which is seen at the bottom through such properties; namely, the property that the fixed stays above the volatile. It is always believed that the volatile seeks to flee by the "points of fire," but when the fixed cause in its own inner parts holds the volatile chained <note>original: "concatenatum"—literally linked together like a chain</note>, never afterward can it escape from its embrace. 

And if you wish to know the proper difference and distance between **fixation** and **congelation** <term>congelatio|The thickening or solidifying of a liquid substance, often compared to the way water freezes into ice or wax hardens.</term>—the difference is found in the action itself. This action provides the instrument which the good **artist** <note>original: "artista"—a common term for the practicing alchemist</note> employs in the manner of working with a well-graduated fire. For without a **gentle fire** <note>original: "ignis leneia"—likely a transcription of "ignis lenitate," meaning a soft, low-intensity heat</note>, the substance cannot be totally fixed.

And if the matter were not by itself with separated matter—so that in **sublimating** <term>sublimatio|A process of purification where a solid is heated into a vapor and then collected as a solid again, leaving impurities behind.</term> something of the volatile cause to shorten the work of fixation—provided that you are not delayed by a great span of time, which nature requires for its work, it will be able to be fixed in the said water as much as you wish. 

But in the end, it will never be deeply fixed; indeed, you will perceive that the last part congealed will be less fixed than the preceding, because if you wished in **F. S.** <note>Likely an abbreviation for "Fixed Sulfur" or "Ferment of Sulfur."</note> which is **sulfur**...

<summary>This page explains the theoretical relationship between congelation (solidifying) and fixation (stabilizing), describing how a "fixed" substance acts as a physical anchor that prevents "volatile" spirits from evaporating when heated. It emphasizes the necessity of a "gentle fire" and patience, noting that nature cannot be rushed in this delicate process.</summary>

<keywords>fixation, congelation, volatile vs fixed, alchemical fire, sublimation, sulfur, mercury, laboratory practice</keywords>

--- Page 314 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of fixation from the previous page. While the previous section established the theory that "the fixed stays above the volatile," this page provides the practical sequence of operations. It utilizes a symbolic alphabet (K, L, M, G, F) common in Lullian alchemy to describe the interactive stages of hardening and stabilization. The text is in Latin, which I have translated into English for accessibility.</meta>

To achieve **fixation** <term>fixatio|The process of making a volatile substance stable in heat so that it no longer evaporates or "flees" the fire.</term>, it will be necessary for you to congeal the substance by fire through a great transformation, as we mentioned to you above in the introduction. Therefore, once the **water** <note>original: "aqua"—likely referring to the "mercurial water" or solvent used in the work</note> has been frozen in a measured form, you must afterward place **L** upon **K**—that is, to be congealed. For just as in state **F**, **G** is fixed by **K**, so in state **F**, the whole of **K** is congealed by **G**. 

Therefore, through the dissolution of **G**, they flow into **K**; in this way, **L** is subsequently congealed. And as **L** congeals, **K** is fixed in such a proportion that it resists fire more effectively than it did previously during its initial **congelation** <term>congelatio|The thickening or solidifying of a liquid substance, often compared to the way water freezes into ice or wax hardens.</term>. 

Thus **L** is frozen, and **M** is placed upon **L**; so **M** is frozen, and **L** is fixed after its freezing—and **K** becomes even stronger than **L**. Then, namely, **L** is fixed because **M** is only capable of being congealed; thus the work of this fixation proceeds, and likewise for **G**. 

Wherefore you can perceive for yourself that after **L** is congealed, it is fixed through the power of **idrotions** <note>original: "idrotionum"—likely a corruption of "hydrationum," referring to watery applications or a specific alchemical 'sweating' process</note> and **imbibitions** <term>imbibitio|The process of gradually adding liquid to a dry substance until it is saturated, like a sponge soaking up water.</term>. And just as has been described regarding **K**, you should understand the same for the other letters. 

This fixation cannot be achieved through **roasting** <term>assatio|The alchemical process of heating a substance in its own moisture or in a dry vessel, similar to roasting over a fire.</term> alone, unless the work involved dissolution; for it is necessary to provide imbibitions. Indeed, in a moist environment, every movement occurs in such a way that only...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on the mechanics of fixation and transformation. For the cause of a **Contrary** <term>contrarium|In alchemical philosophy, "contraries" are opposing qualities such as hot and cold, or dry and moist. This is required by philosophy <note>original: *philosophia*</note> as given through authorities who say: **"Opposites placed beside each other shine more clearly"** <note>original: *opposita iuxta se posita magis elucescunt*. This was a common medieval scholastic maxim used to explain how contrast clarifies the nature of things.</note>. 

And because they are mutual Contraries, their operations are also contrary; for the final goal of the sequence and of the immediate operation of the **fixed** <term>fixum|A substance that has been made stable and no longer evaporates when exposed to heat.</term> is **volatility**, and the proper state of the **volatile** <term>volatile|A substance that vaporizes or "flies" easily in the heat of the fire.</term> is **fixation**. 

Therefore, let us create a **Concordance of Contrariety** <note>The alchemical goal of harmonizing opposing forces so they no longer fight but work together.</note> through an artificial operation in this way: since from a plurality, a living unity is thus made through a **mixture** <note>original: *mistionem*</note> that agrees with the nature and the way of nature. 

Then, understand the cause, because sometimes it is fixed from within. Let the **Volatile Body** <term>corpus volatile|The physical matter of the work that is prone to evaporation before it is "tamed" by the spirit.</term> be made volatile, both parts corresponding to one certain limit. For just as the **spirit** <term>spiritus|The subtle, active essence or gas released during alchemical heating, often associated with Mercury.</term> shall be fixed by its strong nature, so also the body is made volatile by its own nature. By this volatility, it corresponds to the measure of the spirit's fixation; and the fixation of the spirit corresponds to the measure of the bodies' volatility, in such a way that the whole is fixed and the whole is volatile. 

Thus, see by the power of different **measures** <note>The specific proportions or degrees of heat and substance used in the process.</note> that we cause the body to become more fixed than before by **digesting** <term>digere|In alchemy, "digestion" refers to the process of exposing a substance to gentle, constant heat over a long period, similar to how food is broken down in a stomach.</term> it well.

## The Form of the Mixable Elements

The form of the **mixture** <note>original: *mistio*</note> that occurs during the first mixing prepares the mixable elements to receive the "form of the body." This happens through a "specific difference"—a unique identifying quality. For this reason, we believe it is better to judge the process by the skill of the practitioner's hand <note>original: *habilis manus*</note> in managing the **prepared Stone** and the **prepared ferment**, the **thickened water** <note>original: *aqua Inspissata*</note>, and the **refined oil**. These elements of the final mixture rely on this specific difference in their method of creation.

For earth, water, and oil cannot be bound together into a unified whole <note>original: *unitate reductionis*</note> easily. This is because the dryness of the **earth** "kills" <note>original: *mortificat*. In alchemy, to "mortify" a substance is to strip it of its active qualities or change its state through chemical reaction.</note> the **oil**, while the **watery phlegm** <term>phlegma aquosum|A cold, moist, and sluggish byproduct or quality associated with water.</term> and the cold stifle the heat, as it is a substance utterly "desolated" <note>original: *desolata*. This suggests a state where the active qualities have been neutralized or brought to a void state.</note> to the highest degree.

# Recapitulation of the Mastery in an Abbreviated Summary

You must know and understand that the **formative power** <note>original: *virtus formativa*; the vital force believed to shape matter into specific forms.</note> existing in the **seminal matter** <note>original: *materia seminalis*; the fundamental substance containing the potential for growth or transformation.</note> is enclosed in heat and spirit as in simple matter. And although heat, together with the said power, is the **soul** <note>original: *anima*; in alchemical theory, the animating principle or vital heat within the chemical mixture.</note>—not as the action of a physical organic body carrying the power of life within itself—it is rather like a blacksmith in his workshop, forging his nail or another object according to the design in his mind. 

Because of this, it is called the "maker" and "shaper" of its **passive moisture** <note>original: *humidum passivum*; the fluid component that receives the form or "stamp" of the active principles.</note>. This is because, through the act of making, it is introduced by **sealing** <note>original: *sigillationem*; this refers to the "imprinting" of qualities onto the matter, much like a signet ring pressing into wax.</note> and causes a transition through various appropriate colors according to its own sense and understanding, through its own acts and proper operations: by **digesting** <term>Digesting: the process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat, similar to incubation.</term>, limiting, and condensing according to its nature. 

Then you shall have knowledge of its colors and all their causes, and you shall know through natural investigation the proper motions of its nature, with the scientific administration of a **gently governed fire** <note>A "slow fire" or "lamp fire" (ignis lentus), which was essential for the delicate early stages of the Work to avoid "burning" the flowers of the stone.</note> through art, according to the requirements of the essence of things, without excess.

<summary>This page provides a philosophical summary of the "Mastery," comparing the internal formative power of alchemical matter to a blacksmith who shapes substance through heat and intellect. It emphasizes the necessity of gentle fire and the observation of color changes during the processes of digestion and condensation.</summary>

### The Practice of the Whole Mastery Written in General

### Method of the white earth without metallic brightness

This earth drinks and receives an excess of **quicksilver** <term>Quicksilver: mercury (*argentum vivum*), considered the "mother" of all metals and the primary solvent in alchemy.</term> because it is its radical generator <note>original: *Generans Radicalis*; the root source from which the substance naturally grows.</note> in its entire nature, and this can be dissolved through continued **digestion** <term>Digestion: the alchemical process of applying gentle, consistent heat to a substance over a long period to mature or dissolve it.</term>. 

We must understand later—namely, after the separation of its aforementioned "covering" <note>original: *Coopertoris*; a metaphorical outer layer or impurity that must be removed for the metal to reach its pure state.</note>—how to recognize that separation through its **calcination** <term>Calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "ash," used here as a test for purity.</term>. For instance, by taking a small portion and placing it upon coals or upon a heated metal plate: if it should produce black dregs <note>original: *fecem*; residue or impurities.</note>, and unless it were converted into white, it still holds onto some of its said covering, which prevents the reception of the quicksilver as if it were restrained from its own true nature. 

However, if it has become white, it will receive the quicksilver, and you shall repeat the aforementioned **regimen** <note>original: *Regimen*; the specific set of instructions or the "rule" of the operation.</note> as many times as necessary until it passes through a coarse filter with the repetition of the calcination. Then you will see the quicksilver dissolve into a **celestial color** <note>original: *Colore Coelesti*; a sky-blue or heavenly hue, often seen as a sign of successful purification.</note>. 

Next, you shall place that which has been dissolved in your calcination into a separate vessel, and in this way **sublime** <term>Sublime: to heat a solid so that it turns into vapor and then back into a solid, a method of purification.</term> or **distill** <term>Distill: to evaporate a liquid and condense its vapor, separating the "spirit" from the "body."</term> the present watery moisture from the earthly substance, so that brightness and splendor may be created in our **Stone** <term>Our Stone: the Philosopher's Stone (*Lapis*), the ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to be capable of transmuting metals and granting longevity.</term> just as...

### On the Three Humors in the Composition of the
### Spirit of Quicksilver

The explanation of the property of each of the said substances clearly demonstrates there that in the composition of Quicksilver <note>original: *Argentum Vivum*; literally "living silver," the Latin name for mercury.</note>, as it is seen in its own mine, there are specific humors <term>Humors: based on ancient medicine, these are the fluidic qualities or "juices" that determine the nature of a substance.</term>. The first is cold and phlegmatic, sharing the nature of the first earth, which is only <gloss>said to be</gloss> a complete oily moisture. This is expanded in a broad dissolution and is stronger in that oily subtlety itself, sharing the nature of the third earthy substance. This is what causes the refinement <note>original: *subtilitatem*; refers to making a substance thinner, purer, or more volatile.</note> within the substance in which it exists without it rising up <note>original: *sine insurrectione*; likely meaning without premature sublimation or agitation during heating.</note>. The second is the moderate radical humor <term>Radical humor: the fundamental, life-sustaining moisture within a substance, similar to "vital sap."</term>, cultivated and imbued in the essential parts of the second earth, from which it is never separated. And from these two intermediate substances consists the immediate mercurial nature in its pure radical state, and our volatile, incombustible, and white quicksilver. From this, every cause of germinating and coming into being must take its beginning.

<summary>This page describes the three fundamental substances hidden within the alchemical "earth": a heavy/cold impurity, a hot/dry sulfurous principle, and a balanced "middle earth" or vapor. It explains that the interaction of the first two allows for the birth of Quicksilver, while the third provides the essential radical moisture for the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<keywords>three substances, radical moisture, calispina, quicksilver, philosopher's stone, elemental qualities, sulfur, alchemical generation</keywords>

--- Page 336 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the preparation of mercury. It follows the discussion of the three internal substances (sulfur, quicksilver, and the coarse earth) and now focuses on the practical method of purification through sublimation using vitriol and salt, while framing these operations within the philosophical "idioms" or symbolic languages of the alchemists.</meta>

The **philosophers** <note>original: *phi*, a standard abbreviation for *philosophi*.</note> have spoken regarding the immaculate metallic properties to this effect: that one must separate this substance through **sublimation** <term>Sublimation: A process where a solid substance is heated into vapor and then condensed back into a purified solid, leaving impurities behind.</term> by adding two substances, which are **vitriol** <term>Vitriol: A sulfate mineral (like iron or copper sulfate), highly valued in alchemy for its ability to dissolve and transform metals.</term> and **salt**. [The Master] Meritus openly told you that if you wish to know the way of perfection, you must extract the pure substance of **mercury** <term>Mercury: Here referring to the "Philosophical Quicksilver," the living spirit of the metal, rather than common quicksilver.</term> by the power of glass vessels (this is the **sublimation of mercury**) because our **stone** <term>Lapis noster: "Our Stone," referring to the Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of the work.</term> consists of this substance.

Therefore, understand these two **idioms** <note>original: *idioma*; here referring to the specific symbolic languages or conceptual frameworks used by different alchemical traditions.</note> through all that is asserted regarding the properties of the substance of things; for unless the thing itself is understood through its **separated substance**, you will never truly understand the properties of things or of the idioms. This is because the philosophers speak clearly in the **philosophy** <note>original: *phia*, an abbreviation for *philosophia*.</note> of things according to nature. 

For philosophy is nothing other than the telling of the truth of nature's secrets in its own natural idiom, without which the substance of nature cannot be identified—no more than any other cause [can be known] without its **essence** <note>original: *Cossia*; likely a Latinization of the Greek *ousia*, meaning essence or fundamental being.</note> or a plant [known] unless it carries the name and the effect which generally provides knowledge of the nature of the cause. Therefore, diverse idioms were established in nature by various philosophers; thus, each one spoke as clearly as he could within his own philosophy.

<summary>This page describes the purification of mercury through sublimation with vitriol and salt to create the base of the Philosopher's Stone. It also explains that alchemy is a "natural idiom" or language that reveals the hidden essence (ousia) of physical matter.</summary>

<keywords>sublimation, Mercury, Vitriol, Salt, Philosopher's Stone, Philosophy, Essence, Alchemical Language</keywords>

--- Page 337 ---
<meta>This page concludes the treatise. It transitions from the specific chemical operations of purifying mercury to a philosophical justification of the "idioms" or symbolic languages used by alchemists. It reassures the student that the cryptic nature of these texts is a divine and educational necessity rather than an attempt to deceive.</meta>

...according to the **course of nature** <note>original: *cursum nãae* (naturae).</note>, while preserving the properties of their own forms. Although one **idiom** <note>original: *idioma*; referring to the specific symbolic languages or conceptual frameworks used by different alchemical traditions.</note> may be clearer and more appropriate than another—insofar as the author employs a more refined art—it demonstrates the effects of certain properties to every well-meaning **natural intelligence** <note>Refers to a mind that is attuned to the laws of the natural world.</note> with great utility. 

Nor should you believe that the **holy and good fathers** <note>original: *sancti et boni pñi* (patres). In alchemy, "the fathers" are the revered masters of the past, such as Hermes Trismegistus, Geber, or Rhazes.</note> have hidden the true meaning from you in their **philosophy** <note>original: *phĩa* (philosophia).</note>. For this was not done [to deceive]; every property of their living philosophy, once understood, is seen with a clear intention. Rather, for the sake of their own clarity and for the benefit of the dedicated student, they provided both concealment and revelation, even while appearing to speak without any concealment at all. 

To such a student, these future instruments appear in perfect speech, crafted for a **pure intellect** and granted by the **celestial God**. By this process, the **virtue** <term>Virtue: From the Latin *virtus*, meaning the inherent power, strength, or spiritual essence of a substance.</term> of the contrary is examined and purified, just as **gold** is refined in its own **furnace**.

->The End.<-

<summary>This concluding page explains that the cryptic "idioms" of alchemy are intended to guide the student toward a pure understanding granted by God, likening the purification of the mind to the refining of gold in a furnace.</summary>
<keywords>course of nature, idioms, holy fathers, concealment, revelation, pure intellect, gold, furnace, virtue, termination</keywords>
